# Changelog

All notable changes to Open Kioku are documented in this file.
This project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

---

## [Unreleased]

### Breaking

- `open_kioku_core::PruneReason` gains `MsbuildOutput` (serialized `msbuild_output`, label `msbuild-output`) and `open_kioku_core::DependencyEvidence` gains `NugetPackages` (serialized `nuget_packages`, label `nuget-packages`) and `NugetGlobalPackages` (`nuget_global_packages`, `nuget-global-packages`); an exhaustive `match` on either must handle them. `PruneReason::counts_tracked_source` is true for `MsbuildOutput`. `open_kioku_languages::likely_generated_path` is new. Discovery prunes a `bin` beside an MSBuild project file (`*.csproj`, `*.fsproj`, `*.vbproj`) that holds build artifacts, and an `obj` holding a NuGet restore's output or, beside a project file, a `Debug`/`Release` directory, so the JSON and scripts MSBuild copies there (`appsettings.json`, `*.deps.json`) are no longer indexed. `PROJECT_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `project-resolver-v5`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`. `open_kioku_core::is_secret_like_path` now matches `*.snk` and a `secrets.json` below a `UserSecrets` directory, so such a `secrets.json`, indexed with its values redacted before, is no longer read (#684).
- `open_kioku_core::TestTargetOrigin` gains `TestFileHelper` (serialized `test_file_helper`), `open_kioku_core::TestExclusionReason` gains `Helper` (serialized `helper`) and `open_kioku_core::QualityNoteKind` gains `TestDiscovery` (serialized `test_discovery`); an exhaustive `match` on any of them must handle the variant. `TestExclusionReason::tier_justification`, `HELPER_TEST_TARGET`, `every_test_target_excluded`, `withheld_test_file_callables`, `withheld_test_file_callables_in_index`, `open_kioku_context::withheld_helper_ids_for_task` and `open_kioku_tests::TestSelector::for_changed_path_with_withheld` are new. A callable in a test-path file now carries `test_file_symbol` only when it matches its language's default runner discovery rule, and `test_file_helper` otherwise; `TestTarget::counts_as_validation_evidence` is false for the latter, so `ok status`, `ok tests`, MCP `find_tests_for_change` and `repo_status` count such targets under `excluded.helper` (`quality.excluded_test_targets.helper`) instead of as indexed tests. `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v20`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#495).
- `ok verify --diff`, `ok contract verify` and MCP `verify_change` now refuse a supplied diff whose `diff --git` entries use a custom path prefix (`--src-prefix`/`--dst-prefix`) or whose paths fit no single prefix style, with exit 2 (CLI) or `-32602` (MCP), instead of reading it with the prefixes guessed (#675).
- `open_kioku_core::PruneReason` gains `Submodule` (serialized `submodule`, label `submodule`); an exhaustive `match` on it must handle the variant. In a Git repository, discovery prunes every nested work tree as `submodule`: a checked-out submodule, and also a repository cloned or a worktree added inside this one, which v2 indexes held as this repository's source. `PROJECT_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `project-resolver-v3`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`. The coverage summary says `pruned as a submodule` or `pruned as submodules` when every pruned directory is one, and `pruned as build output, dependencies or submodules`, marking each submodule `(submodule)`, when it lists both (#677).
- `open_kioku_git::DiffFile` gains `binary: bool` (`false` when absent from JSON, omitted when false), set when git compared the file as binary, and `open_kioku_impact::DiffImpact` gains `changed_paths_withheld: usize`. A struct literal that builds either must set the field. `open_kioku_impact::withhold_secret_like_paths` and `open_kioku_git::unified_diff::git_header_paths` are new public functions. `ok impact --since` and MCP `impact_analysis` with `since` no longer name a secret-like changed path (`open_kioku_core::is_secret_like_path`: `.env`, key and keystore files): it is left out of `changed_files`, the reports and `removed_paths_not_indexed`, and counted in `changed_paths_withheld` with a caveat; before, a text change to such a path was listed by name. A rename or copy between a secret-like path and another keeps the other side as a plain addition or deletion, without `old_path` or `rename_score`. With `path` as well, a file renamed to a secret-like path is reported as renamed to a withheld path, without its name. `ok plan`, `ok preflight` and MCP `plan_change` with `since` count such paths in the task text instead of listing them. `open_kioku_git::diff_unified_zero` and `diff_unified_zero_since` now return an error for an entry written without `---`/`+++` lines whose `diff --git` line cannot be split into paths; git does not write one with the prefixes these functions pin (#671).
- `open_kioku_core::IndexCoverage` gains `policy_excluded_dirs_by_language: BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, ExcludedDir>>` and `open_kioku_core::CoverageGap` gains `dependency_files: usize` and `excluded_dirs: Vec<CoverageGapDir>` (each omitted from JSON when empty or zero, and empty or zero when absent). A struct literal that builds either must set the fields, or end with `..Default::default()` for `IndexCoverage`. `ExcludedDir`, `ExcludedDirClass`, `DependencyEvidence`, `CoverageGapDir`, `COVERAGE_GAP_DIRS_LISTED`, `IndexCoverage::record_classified_policy_exclusion`, and `CoverageGap::{source_missing_files, source_missing_share, is_source_majority}` are new. `CoverageGap::summary` and `CoverageGap::caveat`, and so the coverage blockers, caveats and `coverage gap:` lines, now name the gap's main directories with their class (#503).
- `open_kioku_core::RelationshipImpactReads` gains `windows_widened: usize` (`0` when absent from JSON), `open_kioku_impact::DiffImpact` gains `removed_paths_not_indexed: Vec<PathBuf>`, and `open_kioku_impact::ChangeFocus` gains `whole_file: bool`. A struct literal that builds any of them must set the field, or end with `..Default::default()` (#665, #668).
- `ok impact --since` and MCP `impact_analysis` with `since` can return more reports than before for the same diff, and `impact_reports_omitted` can be larger. A file the diff deletes, and the previous path of a rename, is now a reported changed path. Before, a deleted file was skipped and not counted (#668).
- `open_kioku_graph::DependencyRoute` gains `stopped_at_hop_limit: Option<usize>` (omitted from JSON when `None`, `None` when absent): the hop limit a route search stopped at with nodes left to expand, set only when no route was found. A struct literal that builds it must set the field. `DependencyRoute::from_edges` is replaced by `from_search(from, to, search, max_hops)`, which takes the search result so a report cannot drop the limit. `open_kioku_core::strongest_shortest_route` and `open_kioku_storage::GraphStore::shortest_path` return `open_kioku_core::RouteSearch`, a new public type holding `edges` and `stopped_at_hop_limit: bool`, instead of `Vec<GraphEdge>`; a caller reads `.edges`, and a `GraphStore` implementation returns `RouteSearch` (`RouteSearch::default()` for no route). `open_kioku_graph::InMemoryGraph::shortest_path` returns `RouteSearch` too, and `open_kioku_graph::route_hop_limit_caveat` is new, as are `open_kioku_core::UNTYPED_WALK_EXCLUDED_EDGE_TYPES` and `is_untyped_walk_excluded`, the edge types no untyped graph read follows, which both stores now read (#666).
- `open_kioku_config::IndexConfig` gains `keep_dirs: Vec<String>` (empty when absent from `ok.toml`, and not written when empty), so a struct literal that builds `IndexConfig` must set it; `IndexConfig::kept_dirs` and `KEEPABLE_DIR_NAMES` are new. `OkConfig::validate`, and so `load_from_repo`, rejects an entry that is not one repository-relative `build` or `dist` directory. `open_kioku_ingest::path_policy::is_pruned_by_discovery(root, rel)` is replaced by `DiscoveryPruner::new(root, &config)?.is_pruned(rel)`, which applies `keep_dirs`; `open_kioku_ingest::resolver::resolve_imports` takes a `&DiscoveryPruner` in place of the root path, and `ProjectModelDiscovery` gains `discover_in(&DiscoveryPruner)`. `PROJECT_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `project-resolver-v2`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#661).
- Plans no longer list the default forbidden rules `target/**`, `build/**` and `dist/**`. Each directory the index's coverage record names as pruned is forbidden instead, as `<path>/**` at any depth with the evidence ref `coverage:pruned:<path>`, except an undeclared build directory holding committed source. A consumer that matched those three patterns by name reads the pruned directories' rules. Where no such directory existed when the repository was indexed (a fresh clone before its first build), an edit under a root `target/`, `build/` or `dist/` is no longer a `forbidden_boundary` violation in `ok verify`: it is `out_of_boundary`, which needs expansion evidence like any edit outside `allowed_files`, and evidence can admit it. No name rule replaces the old ones (#661).
- `open_kioku_core::IndexCoverage::pruned` now stores every pruned directory, not the first 50, so a plan forbids edits under each; `record_pruned_dirs` no longer truncates, and `pruned_unlisted` in the stored record counts only secret-like directories. Status summaries (`ok --json status`, MCP `repo_status` without `detail: "full"`, both the top-level `coverage` and `quality.coverage`) show the first `PRUNED_DIRS_LISTED` through the new `IndexCoverage::status_view`, the rest counted in `pruned_unlisted`; `--full` returns all. `IndexCoverage::record_indexed_undiscovered` and `open_kioku_ingest::path_policy::record_pruned_indexed_files` are new, and `DiscoveryPruner::pruned_at` names the directory that prunes a path (#661).
- `ok --json path` prints the route report MCP `dependency_path` returns, an object `{from, to, edges, edge_authority, route_authority, hop_route_authority, caveats}` (`hop_route_authority` and `caveats` only when a hop is capped), instead of the bare edge array; it lacks only MCP's `evidence_source`. A parser that read the array reads `.edges` (#660).
- `open_kioku_graph::query::GraphQueryResult` gains `paths: Vec<GraphQueryPath>`, one per row, and `open_kioku_graph::query::EdgeRangeExpr` gains `variable: Option<String>`; `GraphQueryPath` and `GraphQueryHop` are new public types. A struct literal that builds either must set the field (#651). The query language's default is unchanged: every edge is matched whatever its authority, so no documented query returns fewer rows.
- `open_kioku_core::ImpactReport` gains `proven_impact_omitted`, `proven_impact_omitted_files` and `possible_impact_omitted_files` (`usize`, omitted from JSON when zero) and `relationship_impact_reads: Option<RelationshipImpactReads>` (omitted when `None`); `RelationshipImpactReads` is a new public type. A struct literal that builds `ImpactReport` must set them. `open_kioku_core::AnalysisFact` gains `ambiguity: Vec<String>` (empty when absent from JSON, omitted when empty), which a struct literal must set. `open_kioku_storage::GraphStore` gains `edge_counts_for_nodes`, with a default that returns `Unsupported`, and `EdgeCountsByNode` is a new public type alias. `open_kioku_impact::ChangeFocus`, `ImpactRequest`, `ImpactAnswer`, `ImpactEngine::for_change`, `ImpactEngine::answer` and `ImpactEngine::diff_focus` are new, and `open-kioku-impact` now depends on `open-kioku-git`. MCP `impact_analysis` no longer requires `path`: it takes `path`, `symbol` or `since`, and with `since` alone it returns `{since, changed_files, impact_reports}` instead of one report. `ok impact --file F --since R` prints F's single report, focused on the lines R changed in it, instead of the `{since, changed_files, impact_reports}` object of every changed file. `ok impact --since` (and MCP `since` alone) reports at most 25 changed paths, adding `impact_reports_omitted` and `caveats` when it leaves some out, and outside a git work tree `--since` is now a usage error (exit 2; `-32602` on MCP) where it returned an empty diff. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v44` and `GRAPH_EMISSION_SEMANTICS_VERSION` `ri3-graph-emission-v5`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#652, #656).
- `open_kioku_core::SyntaxFacts` gains `rust_unit_structs: Vec<SymbolId>`, `rust_type_aliases: Vec<RustTypeAlias>` and `rust_impl_blocks: Vec<RustImplBlock>` (empty when absent from JSON, omitted when empty): a Rust file's unit structs, the type each `type` alias is written to stand for, and each `impl` block's trait and whether it covers every instantiation of its type. `RustTypeAlias` and `RustImplBlock` are new public types. A struct literal that builds `SyntaxFacts` must set the fields or end with `..Default::default()`. A Rust `let` initialized by `Name(..)` now records `inferred_type` `Name()`, and one initialized by a plain path `=path`. `open_kioku_resolution::ScopeIndex` gains `record_rust_type_shapes`, `rust_is_unit_struct`, `rust_alias_target`, `rust_impl_block`, `record_rust_open_trait_scopes` and `rust_trait_scope_is_open`, and `rust_open_trait_scope_files` is a new public function. `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v19` and `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` `ri3-relationship-resolver-v43`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#639, #654).
- `open_kioku_core::AnalysisFact` gains `target_symbol_id: Option<SymbolId>` (`None` when absent from JSON, omitted when unset): the indexed symbol a fact's `target` names, set by the symbol registry. A struct literal that builds `AnalysisFact` must set the field. `open_kioku_core::ImpactReport` gains `possible_impact_omitted: usize` (omitted from JSON when zero) and `relationship_impact_caveats: Vec<String>` (omitted when empty); a struct literal that builds it must set both. `open_kioku_graph::GraphBuffer::holds_edge_above_heuristic` is new (#475).
- `open_kioku_core::IndexCoverage` gains `pruned: Vec<PrunedDir>` and `pruned_unlisted: usize` (both omitted from JSON when empty or zero, and empty or zero when absent), and `PrunedDir` and `PruneReason` are new public types: each directory discovery pruned, by repository-relative path, with its reason (`build_output`, `undeclared_build_dir`, `dependencies`, `virtual_env`) and the git-tracked programming-language files under it. A struct literal that builds `IndexCoverage` must set both or end with `..Default::default()`. `open_kioku_core::SkipReason` gains `Pruned` (serialized `pruned`, not a policy reason); an exhaustive `match` on it must handle the variant. `open_kioku_ingest::path_policy::is_pruned_by_discovery` takes the repository root as a first argument, because the rule now reads marker files on disk (#477).
- The default `[index] exclude` that `ok init` writes, and the patterns `OkConfig::load_from_repo` adds to an existing `ok.toml`, no longer list `target/**`, `**/target/**`, `dist/**`, `**/dist/**`, `build/**` or `**/build/**`, and `open_kioku_languages::likely_vendor_path` no longer matches `target/`, `dist/` or `build/`: discovery decides which of those directories are build output (#477). An `ok.toml` written by an earlier `ok init` still lists the `**/` patterns, which still apply, so a `src/build/` module stays excluded there, now as a visible `config_exclude` skip, until they are removed.
- `open_kioku_plan::select_validation_targets` takes a second argument, a map from test file id to path (`&BTreeMap<FileId, PathBuf>`), used to break ties by repository position; `open_kioku_plan::validation_target_paths` builds it from a `MetadataStore`. A caller passing an empty map gets ties broken on ids alone (#644).
- `open_kioku_core::SyntaxFacts` gains `rust_type_only_items: Vec<SymbolId>` and `rust_enum_variants: Vec<RustEnumVariants>` (empty when absent from JSON, omitted when empty): the Rust braced structs, enums, unions and type aliases a file declares, which are types alone and not also values, and the variants of each enum. `RustEnumVariants` is a new public type. A struct literal that builds `SyntaxFacts` must set the fields or end with `..Default::default()` (#641, #643). `open_kioku_resolution::RustReexport`'s `in_crate` and `from_other_crates` are now `RustReexportNamespaces` (what the name is as a type, `types`, and as a value, `values`) instead of `Option<RustReexported>`; `RustReexportNamespaces` and `RustNamespace` are new public types, and `ScopeIndex` gains `record_rust_type_items`, `rust_in_namespace`, `rust_enum_variants` and `inline_module_body`. `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v18` and `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is `ri3-relationship-resolver-v42` (v41 is left to an open change), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- `open_kioku_core::SyntaxFacts` gains `invokes_item_macro: bool` (`false` when absent from JSON, omitted when false), set for a Rust file whose top level invokes a macro that may expand to items and `use` declarations no other fact records (not `compile_error!`, the `assert!` and `const_assert!` families, `include_str!`, `include_bytes!` or `doctest!`), and `item_macro_names: Vec<String>` (empty when absent from JSON, omitted when empty), the statics a top-level `thread_local!` declares. A struct literal that builds `SyntaxFacts` must set the fields or end with `..Default::default()` (#476). `open_kioku_resolution::RustReexport` and `RustReexported` are new public types, recorded through the new `ScopeIndex::record_rust_reexports`: what each name a Rust module brings in with `use` names, by qualified name. `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v17` and `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is `ri3-relationship-resolver-v40`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- `open_kioku_resolution::RustConfiguredModules` gains `routes: BTreeMap<String, RustModuleRoute>`, `stems: BTreeMap<FileId, String>` and `unread_by: BTreeMap<Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>>`: the route of each file below a configuration choice (the file each module from the choice down to it is compiled from), the path of each indexed file below one, and the files whose `mod` items leave a module's files unknown. `RustModuleRoute` and `RustConfiguredRead` (what a module path names, read from one file, from the new `RustConfiguredModules::read`) are new public types. A struct literal that builds `RustConfiguredModules` must set the fields or end with `..Default::default()`. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v34` (v33 is left to an open change), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`; parser output is unchanged (#624, #625).
- `open_kioku_semantic_model::ImportBinding` gains `configured_targets: Option<ConfiguredImportTargets>` (omitted from JSON when `None`), set on a Rust import whose path, or a `pub use` it is followed through, passes through a module whose file configuration selects: what the path names in each file that may hold that module. `ConfiguredImportTargets` is a new public type. A struct literal that builds `ImportBinding` must set the field (#615).
- `open_kioku_core::Symbol` gains `alias_of: Option<TypeAliasTarget>` (`None` when absent from JSON, omitted when unset), and `TypeAliasTarget` is a new public type: the symbol id and qualified name of the repository type a Go type alias stands for. A struct literal that builds `Symbol` must set the field. `open_kioku_ingest::symbol_registry::RegistryReport` gains `type_alias_targets`, and `symbol_registry::apply_type_alias_targets` is a new public function. The SQLite `symbols` table gains a nullable `alias_order_key` column, added when a store opens an older index (#621); symbol listings now sort by a `list_order_key` column generated from it (#634). `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v37` (v34 is #628's and v36 is #631's; v33 and v35 were reserved for this change and are left unused), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- `open_kioku_core::SyntaxFacts` gains `package_declaration: Option<PackageDeclarationSite>` (`None` when absent from JSON), and `PackageDeclarationSite` is a new public type: the file and the package a Java `package` declaration or a Go `package` clause names. A struct literal that builds `SyntaxFacts` must set the field or end with `..Default::default()`. `open_kioku_ingest::symbol_registry::RegistryScopeModel::new` takes an eighth argument, the `PackageDeclarationSite`s of the indexed files (#617, #620). `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v15` (v14 is taken by an open change), so an index built before this change reports stale parser facts until rebuilt.
- `open_kioku_core::SyntaxFacts` gains `type_aliases: Vec<TypeAliasSite>` (empty when absent from JSON), and `TypeAliasSite` is a new public type: a Go type alias's symbol and the package qualifier and name of the type it stands for. A struct literal that builds `SyntaxFacts` must set the field or end with `..Default::default()`. `open_kioku_ingest::symbol_registry::RegistryScopeModel::new` takes a seventh argument, the `TypeAliasSite`s of the indexed files (#618). `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v13`, so an index built before this change reports stale parser facts until rebuilt.
- `open_kioku_resolution::ResolutionOutcome` gains an `Alternatives { candidates, reason }` variant, for candidates in files of which configuration compiles one; an exhaustive `match` on the enum must handle it (#613). `open_kioku_resolution::RustConfiguredModules` and `RustModuleFiles` are new public types, recorded through the new `ScopeIndex::record_rust_configured_modules`, and `open_kioku_languages::rust` gains `cfg_attr_condition` and `cfg_conditions_hold_on_every_build`. `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v12`, since `ModuleDeclarationSite::path_is_conditional` is now unset for `cfg_attr` conditions that hold on every build; an index built before this change reports stale Rust parser facts until rebuilt.
- `open_kioku_core::ModuleDeclarationSite` gains `path_is_conditional: bool` (`false` when absent from JSON), set when every `path` attribute of a Rust `mod` item is inside a `cfg_attr`; a struct literal that builds it must set the field (#608). `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v11`, so an index built before this change reports stale Rust parser facts until rebuilt.
- `open_kioku_semantic_model::CargoManifest` gains `external_dependencies: Vec<CargoExternalDependency>` (omitted from JSON when empty), the dependencies a manifest places outside the repository (registry, git, or a `path` leaving it, unless the workspace root's `[patch]` or `[replace]` points the package at a `path` in the repository), each with the crate name code writes and its dependency kind; `CargoExternalDependency` is a new public type, re-exported from `open_kioku_ingest::project_model`, and `ProjectModel::rust_external_dependency` a new method. A struct literal that builds `CargoManifest` must set the field or end with `..Default::default()` (#599).
- `open_kioku_semantic_model::CargoManifest` gains `edition: Option<String>` (omitted from JSON when `None`), the package's Rust edition as its manifest gives it or inherits it from `[workspace.package]`; `None` for a virtual manifest or an edition the index cannot read. A struct literal that builds `CargoManifest` must set the field or end with `..Default::default()` (#632).
- `open_kioku_storage_sqlite::GraphReconciliation` gains `nodes_rewritten: usize`, the stored graph nodes an incremental update rewrote because the new graph holds them under the same id with different content; a struct literal that builds it must set the field or end with `..Default::default()` (#591).
- `open_kioku_storage_sqlite::GraphReconciliation` gains `edges_rewritten: usize`, the stored edges an incremental update rewrote because the new graph holds them under the same id with different content; a struct literal that builds it must set the field or end with `..Default::default()` (#581).
- `open-kioku-core`: `IndexQuality` gains `pending_derived_store_pruning: bool` (omitted from JSON when false, `false` when absent), so a struct literal that builds `IndexQuality` must set it or end with `..Default::default()`. A failed prune of the semantic vector store or stored context handles is now recorded there instead of in `pending_deleted_content_clearing`, which again means only the database's own compaction; a client that read `pending_deleted_content_clearing` to learn about the derived stores reads the new field (#585).
- `open_kioku_semantic_model::ProjectRoot` gains `cargo_manifest: Option<CargoManifest>` (omitted from JSON when `None`): the package name, proc-macro flag, workspace members and repository dependencies a parsed `Cargo.toml` declares, as the new `CargoManifest`, `CargoDependency` and `CargoDependencyKind`; a struct literal that builds `ProjectRoot` must set it. `open_kioku_core::ImportSite` gains `reexported: bool` (a Rust `pub use`; omitted from JSON when false), which a struct literal must set. `ImportBindingRule` gains `RustReexport` (#587).
- `open-kioku-core`: `IndexQuality` gains `pending_deleted_content_clearing: bool` (omitted from JSON when false, `false` when absent), so a struct literal that builds `IndexQuality` must set it or end with `..Default::default()` (#553).
- `open_kioku_scip::prepare_and_import_scip` and `import_configured_scip_files` take a fourth argument, `withhold: &dyn Fn(&Path) -> bool`, asked about each SCIP document's repository-relative path; a withheld document contributes no symbol or occurrence. `|_| false` keeps the previous behaviour. `ScipImport`, `ScipImportReport` and `ScipIndexReport` gain `withheld_documents: usize` (defaults to 0 when deserialized), so a struct literal that builds one must set it (#536).
- `ok tests` (with `--json`, and in its default output, which prints the same JSON while it is under 4096 bytes) and MCP `find_tests_for_change` return an object, `{tests, excluded, excluded_sample, caveats}`, instead of a bare array of test targets; MCP's `structuredContent` is that object rather than `{value: [...]}`. A parser that read the array reads `.tests`. MCP `find_tests_for_change` now ranks with the selector `ok tests`, plan, verify and the context pack use (`for_changed_path_with_evidence`, which adds exact symbol-reference overlap), where it used the plainer `for_changed_path`, so its ordering for a given path can change; the two surfaces now return the same list (#494).
- `open-kioku-core`: `IndexQuality` gains `excluded_test_targets: Option<BTreeMap<TestExclusionReason, usize>>`, `None` (omitted from JSON) on a manifest written before it was recorded and `{}` when nothing was excluded; a struct literal that builds `IndexQuality` must set it or end with `..Default::default()`. `TestExclusionReason` is a new public enum (one variant, `Disabled`), so a downstream exhaustive `match` will need to handle variants added later (#494).
- `open_kioku_ingest::symbol_registry::resolve_symbol_edges` takes a fifth argument, `Option<&RegistryScopeModel>`, the resolver's scope and import model built with `RegistryScopeModel::new`; `None` keeps the unscoped same-file match. `open_kioku_resolution::context::rust_rules_out_same_file_item` is now public, so the registry applies the resolver's Rust scoping rule rather than a copy of it (#526).
- `ok verify` and MCP `verify_change` return `fail` (exit 1 on the CLI) for a diff whose hunks disagree with their `@@` headers, with a `malformed_diff` boundary violation naming the entry and diff line; such a diff could verify with exit 0 before. A hunk that declares more lines than follow it (a diff cut mid-hunk, or hand-edited) used to swallow the next plain entry's `---`/`+++` headers, so that file never reached the changed-file set or any boundary or forbidden-path check; a count that did not parse was read as zero; and a hunk that declares fewer lines than it holds left its extra content to be read as headers. All three were silent. A diff cut exactly between two file entries is still well-formed and cannot be detected. Well-formed git output is unaffected. The hunk scanner now lives once, in `open_kioku_git::unified_diff`, and `open-kioku-patch` uses it instead of its own copy (#486).
- `open_kioku_git::commit_patches` returns `CommitPatchScan { commits, skipped }` instead of `Vec<CommitPatch>`; `skipped` lists the commits whose patch could not be read, as `SkippedCommitPatch { commit_id, reason }`. `open-kioku-core`: `QualityNoteKind` gains `GitHistory` (serialized `git_history`), so an exhaustive `match` on it must handle the new variant (#482).
- `ok snapshot import` and `ok index --from-snapshot auto` now refuse an artifact whose commit cannot be related to the local `HEAD`: its commit is not in the repository (fetch it first), shares no history with `HEAD`, was not recorded, or the directory is not a Git work tree with a commit. Previously any structurally valid artifact was imported and served as if it described the checkout. `ok snapshot import --allow-foreign` imports such an artifact marked `foreign`; `ok index --from-snapshot auto` falls back to a full index, as it does for any refused import. Existing users who import into a directory without Git history, or from a repository whose history is not fetched, must pass `--allow-foreign` or run `ok index` (#473).
- `ok snapshot import` removes from the imported index every file and document the importing repository's policy excludes (secret-like and denied paths, hidden files, `[index] exclude`, `.gitignore`, `.okignore`), and every Git history row naming a secret-like or denied path, before publishing it. An import from an exporter with a broader policy now serves fewer files than the artifact holds; the removed files are recorded in the manifest's coverage and skipped paths as policy exclusions, the import report gives the count per rule under `policy_filtered_by_source`, and `snapshot.policy_filtered` records the total. An artifact whose rows disagree with each other where readers rely on them to agree (a path column that differs from the path in the row's JSON, a row that belongs to no indexed file, a graph dictionary entry under the wrong hash) is refused, with or without `--allow-foreign`; `ok snapshot export` never produces one. SCIP symbols and occurrences for files discovery skipped are the writer's own state and are accepted, then removed by the policy step (#473, #536).
- `open_kioku_resolution::RustModulePlacement` gains `own_subtree_in_every_crate: bool`: with `in_other_crates` set, every crate that may compile the file compiles it at the place its module path spells, so a `self::`/`super::` path ending below the file's own module is still read. A struct literal that builds `RustModulePlacement` must set it (`true` for a file no other crate compiles) (#576).
- `open_kioku_core::ModuleDeclarationSite` gains `path_attributes: Vec<String>`, the string literals of the `mod` item's `path` attributes (defaults to empty when deserialized), and `open_kioku_resolution::RustModulePlacement` gains `in_other_crates: bool`; a struct literal that builds either must set the new field (#572).
- `open_kioku_semantic_model::ProjectRoot` gains `cargo_targets: CargoTargets`, the binary, integration-test, example and bench crate roots a Cargo manifest names with `[[bin]]`, `[[test]]`, `[[example]]` and `[[bench]]` tables and the target kinds whose auto-discovery `autobins = false` and its peers turn off (omitted from JSON when empty). `CargoTargets` and `CargoTargetKind` are new public types, re-exported from `open_kioku_ingest::project_model`. A struct literal that builds `ProjectRoot` must set `cargo_targets: CargoTargets::default()` (#544).
- `open_kioku_semantic_model::ProjectRoot` gains `library_root: Option<PathBuf>`, the library crate root a Cargo manifest sets with `[lib] path` (`None` for the default `src/lib.rs`, and omitted from JSON when `None`); `ProjectRoot` does not implement `Default`, so a struct literal that builds it must set `library_root: None` (#543).
- `open-kioku-core`: `IndexManifest` gains `snapshot: Option<SnapshotProvenance>` (serialized only when present). A struct literal that builds `IndexManifest` must set `snapshot: None` (#473).
- `open_kioku_ingest::resolver::resolve_imports` takes a fifth argument, `&RustImportEdgeTargets`, carrying what each Rust `use` path of the importing file's own crate names; `open_kioku_ingest::imports::RustImportEdgeTargets::record` is public so a caller outside the crate can build one. `Default::default()` compiles and means every in-crate Rust path is unresolved, which is a degraded answer rather than an error, so a caller that indexes Rust should populate it rather than pass the default (#458).
- `open-kioku-core`: `ConfidenceSignalInput` gains `coverage: CoverageInput` and `primary_language_keys: Vec<String>`. A struct literal without `..Default::default()` must add both; `CoverageInput::default()` (a recorded record with no gaps) and an empty vector keep the previous scoring. `CoverageInput` has three variants - `Recorded(Vec<CoverageGap>)`, `Unavailable` for an index that published no coverage record, and `Unreadable` for a record that could not be read - so a downstream `match` on it must cover all three.
- `ok search --json` prints `{results, truncated, warnings, caveats}` in the ranked modes (`code`, `graph`, `--semantic`, `--hybrid`), the shape `ok search --regex --json` already printed, instead of a bare array of results. `truncated` and `warnings` carry the filled-candidate-window report MCP `search_code` returns for the same state, in the same words, so neither surface can claim an answer is complete while the other does not; the human output prints that sentence as a `warning:` line. A parser that read the array reads `.results` instead (#448).
- `repo_status` and `ok --json status` return `quality.quality_notes` as `{total, by_kind, sample}` and `quality.skipped_paths` as `{total, by_reason, sample}` (previously full lists of quality notes and skipped paths), each `sample` at most 20 entries drawn round-robin across kinds or reasons, instead of the full lists; `detail: "full"` (MCP) and `ok status --full` (JSON, Markdown, and text) return every entry, and the manifest still stores them all. Each quality note is now `{kind, message}` with the kind assigned by its producer (`discovery`, `scip`, `exact_references`, `index_mode`, `import_resolver_caveat`, `import_resolver_cap`, `symbol_registry_caveat`, `symbol_registry_unresolved`, `relationship_resolution`); notes stored by an earlier index deserialize as `unclassified`. Measured on this 380-file repository after a full index: `ok --json status` went from 1,771,248 bytes (9,767 notes, 3,286 skipped paths) to 24,392 bytes with the summaries (`--full`: 2,577,421 bytes, the typed notes being larger than the bare strings).
- `ok verify` without `--git`, `--diff`, `--changed` or `--since-plan` is a usage error with exit code 2, a diff that names no file exits 2 with `invalid input: …` (`OkError::InvalidInput`), and `verify_change` returns JSON-RPC `-32602` for the same input (previously exit 1 and `-32000`).
- `open-kioku-core`: `IndexCoverage::record_policy_exclusion` now takes the file's language as its first argument, so `policy_excluded_by_language` is recorded with `policy_excluded_by_source` and cannot drift from it. Migrate `coverage.record_policy_exclusion(source, top_dir)` to `coverage.record_policy_exclusion(&language, source, top_dir)`.
- `open-kioku-core`: `IndexQuality` gains `redacted_files: Option<usize>` and `pending_pre_redaction_compaction: bool`; a struct literal that builds `IndexQuality` must set both or end with `..Default::default()`. Both are serialized always: `redacted_files` is `null` rather than omitted on a manifest written before redaction, so a client reading `quality.redacted_files ?? 0` cannot render an index that stored values as read as one with nothing to redact.
- `open-kioku-core`: `ConfidenceSignalInput` gains `named_anchor_count`, `unmatched_anchors` and `weak_anchors` and no longer derives `Copy`; `named_anchors`, `unmatched_named_anchors` and `weak_named_anchors` are new public functions (compared with 4.0.0).
- `open-kioku-ranking`: `RankingOptions` gains `text_relevance_scale` (default `Raw`, no behaviour change); breaking only for crates that build `RankingOptions` with a struct literal without `..Default::default()`. The new `TextRelevanceScale` enum is `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a downstream `match` on it needs a wildcard arm.
- `open_kioku_core::SearchResult` has a new public field, `exact_reference_provenance: Option<EvidenceSourceType>`, so struct-literal construction outside the workspace no longer compiles. Set `exact_reference_provenance: None` on results that are not produced from an indexed symbol occurrence (`SearchResult` does not implement `Default`). The field deserializes as `None` when absent and is omitted from JSON when `None` (#438).
- `open_kioku_impact::is_exact_reference_result` is removed; use `SearchResult::is_exact_reference()`, which reads the typed provenance instead of the `match_reason` prefix (#438).
- `open_kioku_core::SyntaxFacts` gains the public field `module_declarations: Vec<ModuleDeclarationSite>`, and `open_kioku_semantic_model::ImportBinding` gains `rule: ImportBindingRule` (a `#[non_exhaustive]` enum), so downstream code that builds either with a struct literal stops compiling; `SyntaxFacts::default()` and `..Default::default()` are unaffected, and serialized data without the fields still deserializes (#453).
- Caller-argument mistakes are `OkError::InvalidInput`: JSON-RPC `-32602` on MCP and exit code 2 on the CLI. A plan the tool generated itself that cannot become a contract, and any failure of the index, store or filesystem, stay `-32000` and exit 1. Each case, previous → now:
  - Blank `search_code` query, blank `ok search` query: `-32000`, exit 1 → `-32602`, exit 2. Both now say `search requires a non-empty query` (previously `` `search_code` requires a non-empty `query` `` and `` `ok search` requires a non-empty query ``).
  - Unknown `retrieve_context` or `ok retrieve-context` handle: `-32000`, exit 1 → `-32602`, exit 2.
  - Unknown `contract_id` on `verify_change`, and unknown id on `ok contract verify --id`, `ok contract explain --id`, `ok contract show` and `ok contract export`: `-32000`, exit 1 → `-32602`, exit 2, with `` invalid input: no contract `<id>` is stored for this repository; … `` (previously `Contract not found: <id>`). A `get_definition` or `get_references` name the index does not hold stays `-32000`: it reports index content, not a bad argument.
  - Unknown `detail` on `repo_status` or `plan_change`: `-32000` → `-32602`. `plan_change` checks it before the relationship-evidence check, and `repo_status` rejects it on a repository that is not indexed, where it was ignored.
  - Plan the contract builder rejects for lacking evidence references or files, when supplied (`plan_change` `plan` or `plan_json` with `persist: true`; `ok contract create --plan` or `--plan-json`): `-32000`, exit 1 → `-32602`, exit 2.
  - Unknown `mode` on `search_code`, unknown `kind` on `get_references`: `-32000` → `-32602`. The CLI equivalents are clap values and already exited 2.
  - Missing required argument, or argument of the wrong JSON type, on any tool: `-32000` → `-32602`.
  - `plan`, `plan_json`, `contract`, `contract_json`, `verification`, `verification_json` or `validation_attestations` that does not decode: `-32000` → `-32602`. A plan file that reads but does not decode (`ok verify --plan`, `ok verify-boundary --plan`, `ok contract create --plan`), `--plan-json`, and `--contract` or `--contract-json` that does not decode: exit 1 → exit 2. A file that cannot be read still exits 1.
  - Selector given none or several of its inputs (`task`/`plan`/`plan_json` with `persist: true`; `contract_id`/`contract`/`contract_json`; the CLI's TASK/`--plan`/`--plan-json` and `--id`/`--contract`/`--contract-json`), and `write_attestation` or `--write-attestation` without a stored contract: `-32000`, exit 1 → `-32602`, exit 2.
  - Unknown `format` on a contract output or verification explanation: `-32000` → `-32602`.
  - Blank `regex_search` pattern: `-32000` → `-32602`. `regex_search` or `ok search --regex` pattern that does not parse: `-32000`, exit 1 → `-32602`, exit 2, with `invalid input: regex parse error: …` (previously `search error: regex parse error: …`); the `regex_search_invalid_pattern.json` golden snapshot moves with it.
  Every message above gains the `invalid input: ` prefix. In `tools/list`, the descriptions of `repo_status` and `plan_change` `detail`, `search_code` `mode`, `regex_search` `pattern` and `get_references` `kind` say invalid params instead of tool error. (#465)
- `open-kioku-core`: `BoundaryFileRule` gains `evidence_refs_omitted: usize`; a struct literal without `..Default::default()` must set it. Serialized rules without the field still deserialize, with a count of 0.
- `open-kioku-core`: `ImpactReport` gains `direct_impacts_omitted: usize` and `indirect_impacts_omitted: usize`; a struct literal must set both (`ImpactReport` does not implement `Default`). Each is omitted from JSON when 0, and a report serialized without them deserializes with counts of 0 (#497).
- `query_evidence_graph` and `ok graph query` reject as `parse_error` queries they accepted before: `source`, `source_type` or `confidence` on a node variable, `qualified_name` on a File node, and `file_path` or `qualified_name` on a node type that is neither File nor a symbol type (such as `ConfigKey`). `file_path` and `qualified_name` also return different rows: both compared every node's label, and they now read a File node's path, a symbol node's defining file path and a symbol node's qualified name.
- `open-kioku-graph`: `FilterExpr.value` is `FilterValue` (`Text(String)` or `Number(f32)`) instead of `String`, `FilterOperator` gains `LessThan`, `LessOrEqual`, `GreaterThan` and `GreaterOrEqual`, and `Token` gains `NumberLiteral`, `LessThan`, `LessOrEqual`, `GreaterThan` and `GreaterOrEqual`; an exhaustive match on either enum must handle them.
- `open-kioku-core`: `PlanReport` gains `validation_omitted: usize` and `validation_omitted_ids: Vec<String>`; a struct literal that builds `PlanReport` must set both (`0` and an empty vector keep the previous meaning). Both deserialize as zero/empty when absent, so a plan saved before this change still loads. `open_kioku_plan::select_validation_targets` returns `ValidationSelection { selected, omitted_by_cap }` instead of `Vec<TestTarget>`; `.selected` is the previous return value (#496).
- `open-kioku-context`: `ContextPackBuilder::build_from_primary` is removed. It had no caller in the workspace; build packs with `build`, `build_with_budget` or `build_with_sources` (#355).

### Added

- `[index] keep_dirs` in `ok.toml` lists `build` and `dist` directories discovery walks although it would prune them as build output (`keep_dirs = ["tools/dist"]`). Every file under a kept directory still passes the secret-path rule, `[paths] deny`, the hidden-file rule and the ignore files. `ok init` does not write the key; `ok doctor` names an entry discovery never reaches, and its next step for committed source under an undeclared build directory now names the key (#661).
- `open_kioku_graph::DependencyRoute` and `dependency_route` build the route report `ok path` and MCP `dependency_path` share, with `DEPENDENCY_ROUTE_MAX_HOPS` and `ROUTE_CONTAINMENT_DESCENT_CAVEAT`, and `open_kioku_core::strongest_shortest_route` is the walk both graph stores use for `shortest_path` (#660).
- `ok graph query` and MCP `query_evidence_graph` report the authority of the edges each row was matched through, and can filter on it (#651). Since #475 a symbol-registry name match ends at the resolved symbol's node, so `MATCH (a:Function)-[:CALLS *1..3]->(b:Function)` chained name matches into real symbols, and a route made of heuristic hops read the same as a proven one.
  - Each result has `paths`, one entry per row: the row's edges (`edge_id`, `edge_type`, `from`, `to`, `authority`) and `weakest_authority`, the weakest of them, since a route is only as established as its weakest hop. A multi-hop row reports the strongest route the walk found to its target at that depth: the walk keeps, per node and depth, the route whose weakest hop is strongest, so `ledger::close` reached both through a name match and through two proven calls reads `authoritative`.
  - `authority` is a filter field on a bound edge, taking `=`, `<`, `<=`, `>` and `>=` with `'heuristic'`, `'corroborating'` or `'authoritative'`, in that order. A hop range can now bind a variable, `-[c:CALLS *1..3]->`, and a filter on it holds for every hop: `WHERE c.authority = 'authoritative'` walks proven calls only.
  - Containment does not carry across a relationship: a `CONTAINS` or `DEFINES` hop taken after a relationship hop contributes at most `corroborating` to its route (`open_kioku_core::graph_route_hop_authority`), however established the edge is. `src/ledger.rs` importing `src/audit.rs`, then `src/audit.rs` defining `archive`, reads `corroborating`, not as a proven relation between ledger.rs and `archive`. The hop keeps its own `authority` and adds `route_authority`, its path carries a caveat, and an `authority` filter on a hop range compares that contribution, so it agrees with `weakest_authority`. The walk keeps a route that has not yet crossed a relationship beside one that has, so containment reached first through a relationship does not cap a route that needed none. The converse, a symbol up to its file and across the file's import, is never produced: every route surface follows outgoing edges only, and tests pin that.
  - A caveat counts the returned rows that cross a heuristic edge and says how to leave them out.
  - The default stays every edge, with authority shown, rather than proven edges only. Edge types the proof policy never establishes (`SIMILAR_TO`, `TESTS`, `DERIVED_FROM` and others) are never `authoritative`, so a proven-only default would have turned every query over them into an empty result, which reads as "no such relationship". Weak evidence stays visible and is labelled instead.
- `open_kioku_core::graph_edge_authority` is the authority a surface reports beside an edge: a relationship edge's proof-derived authority, and for `CONTAINS` and `DEFINES`, which resolve no name, `authoritative` when a parser or index extracted the edge and `heuristic` when the regex fallback did, the split bounded edge windows already make (#651).
- An index published by `ok snapshot import` records where it came from: `snapshot: {imported_from_commit, local_commit, relation, commits_behind, commits_ahead, changed_files, policy_filtered}` in the manifest, reported by `ok status` (text, `--json`, `--markdown`), MCP `repo_status`, and a `snapshot` check in `ok doctor` that warns when the index does not describe the checkout. Context packs (`retrieval_diagnostics.caveats`, `confidence_breakdown.caveats`) and plans (`risk.reasons`) carry the same caveat until `ok index` rebuilds from source. `ok snapshot doctor` runs the import's revision check and reports a refusal as an error. New public APIs: `open_kioku_git::{compare_with_head, changed_paths_since_commit, RevisionRelation, RevisionComparison}`, `open_kioku_ingest::path_policy::{IndexPathPolicy, SecurityPathPolicy, record_excluded_indexed_file, redact_recorded_skips}` (the discovery path policy, now shared by `ok index` and the importer; `SecurityPathPolicy` is its secret-like and `[paths] deny` rules alone, which history ingestion also uses), `SqliteStore::{stored_paths, purge_paths, consistency_violations}`, and `MetadataStore::snapshot_provenance()` with a default implementation (#473).
- `open-kioku-storage`: `MetadataStore::index_coverage()` returns the index coverage record without decoding the rest of the manifest, with a default implementation delegating to `manifest()` so existing implementors are unaffected. `SqliteStore` overrides it to read `$.quality.coverage` through `json_extract`, because context packs read coverage on every build and a full decode of a large manifest is dominated by quality notes and skipped paths.
- `repo_status` and `ok --json status` return `coverage_gaps` beside `coverage` whenever coverage was recorded: each programming language whose missing source changes what an absence means, with `language`, `cause` (`git_ignore`, `excluded_by_policy`, `omitted`), `missing_files`, `language_files`, `reason` and `governing_setting`, and `[]` when coverage is complete. `open-kioku-core` adds `CoverageGap`, `CoverageGapCause`, `COVERAGE_GAP_MAJORITY_SHARE`, `IndexCoverage::gaps`, `NegativeEvidence::for_coverage_gaps` and `negative_evidence_scope::COVERAGE`; `open-kioku-context` adds `primary_language_keys`, which plans reuse.
- Files in a data, config, or prose format (YAML, JSON, TOML, Markdown, plain text, and document-corpus files) are indexed with secret-like values replaced by `[REDACTED]` before chunks, symbols, analysis facts, test candidates, and document sections are derived from them, so the values are absent from `.ok/index.sqlite`, the Tantivy index, `ok snapshot export` artifacts, `ok search`, and MCP results; line numbers are unchanged. A value is replaced when its key names a secret, when it is a private-key PEM block or a URL password, or, in YAML, JSON, TOML, Terraform/HCL, and Dockerfiles but not in ordinary Markdown or plain text, when it is a machine-generated-looking token of at least 20 characters; a file whose name says it holds credentials (`docs/SECRETS.md`, `notes/credentials.md`, `secret_key.txt`) is read under the config rules whatever its format, so a token pasted there with no key around it is still replaced; algorithm-labelled digests such as lockfile `sha512-` integrity values, `null`, booleans, bare `${VAR}` references, a value that names where a secret lives rather than holding one, which needs a locator-named key (matched as whole words: `auth_token_env`, `password_file`, `token_path`, not `profile` or `logfile`), a value that parses wholly as a locator (an environment variable name of at least two upper-case segments, or an absolute or `./`-relative path), and nothing machine-generated inside that value — and applies only in config and data formats, where the entropy rule also runs. `password_file: hunter2`, `password_file: 4859`, `password_file: s3cret/pw`, `token_path: a/b`, `secretName: JBSWY3DPEHPK3PXP` and `token_env: TOKEN` are all replaced, and unquoted numbers under a key that names a quantity (`max_tokens: 4096`) are kept, while a number under any other secret-named key (`password: 123456`) is replaced. The semantic vector store is built from the same redacted chunks. Rules, calibration, and limits: `docs/security-model.md`, "Secret-value redaction". Programming-language source is indexed as written. `ok index` and `ok status` print the number of files with redacted values, `ok doctor` gains a `redaction` check (a warning on an index written before redaction, with `ok index .` as the next step), the `ok status --markdown` table gains a `Files with redacted values` row, and `ok --json status` and `repo_status` carry the count as `quality.redacted_files`, serialized as `null` rather than omitted on older manifests so a client cannot read an unredacted index as one with nothing to redact. `ok search`, `ok search --regex`, MCP `search_code` and `regex_search` carry a caveat naming how many files hold redacted values, because a redacted value cannot be found by searching for it; all four render one sentence from `open_kioku_core::redaction_search_caveat`, so the human and agent surfaces report the same state. The `repo_status` tool description states both fields. `ok snapshot export` refuses every mode while an index predates redaction, since its rows hold the values as read and `VACUUM INTO` copies rows; once rebuilt, only `--quality fast` is refused. The first `ok index` over an index written before redaction clears what that index stored as read: it removes the semantic vector store (`.ok/vectors`, whose target text came from unredacted chunks; re-run `ok semantic index` to rebuild it), runs `VACUUM`, and truncates the write-ahead log. The work is recorded rather than assumed: the manifest published by that run carries `quality.pending_pre_redaction_compaction: true`, cleared only once the clearing succeeds, so a blocked run stays outstanding in `ok status` and `repo_status`, `ok doctor` warns, and the next `ok index` retries it. A truncating checkpoint blocked by another connection returns `busy` instead of failing, and that result is now read and treated as a failure. The clearing runs after publication and never fails the index run. `ok watch` rebuilds such an index in full instead of updating it in part, then clears it the same way. `ok snapshot export --quality fast` refuses to copy an index that still owes this work, since it ships the database file with its free pages (#379).
- Per-task-family metrics on the commit-derived benchmarks (`docs/retrieval-benchmark.md`, "Per-task-family breakdown"). `scripts/score-context-cases.py` adds a `by_task_family` section that reports every aggregate metric, its bootstrap interval, and case coverage for each routed family (`retrieval_diagnostics.routing.task_family`, named by `TaskFamily`), and each row records its `task_family`; existing report keys are unchanged. A family with fewer than 34 scored cases is marked `insufficient` and never gated. `scripts/compare-commit-derived-report.py` gates each sufficient family on R@5, R@20, MRR, and `gold_recall@20` with the aggregate's exit status once a baseline carries the section (membership and tolerance rules in the next entry). The baselines carry it from the 2026-09-25 re-freeze (under Changed). The scorer output, the compare output, and the nightly job summary print each family with its 95% intervals under a note that families are the router's labels, and the summary's gate column comes from the compare script. The scripts' unit tests (`scripts/tests/test_context_yield.py`, `scripts/tests/test_commit_derived_families.py`) run in the Benchmarks workflow (#388).
- Per-family membership, tolerance, and a validated freeze for the commit-derived baselines (`docs/retrieval-benchmark.md`, "Per-task-family breakdown"):
  - **Membership.** Each `by_task_family` family records `membership_fingerprint`, `sha256:` and a SHA-256 over the sorted positions of its cases in the split file and the split's case count (no commit hashes, paths, or subjects); `scripts/reduce-benchmark-report.py` allowlists exactly that format.
  - **Gate.** `scripts/compare-commit-derived-report.py` gates a family only when its fingerprint matches the baseline's and prints `membership changed` otherwise. A gated family's tolerance is `max(0.03, 2/n)` for a baseline family of `n` cases (0.0588 at the 34-case minimum, 0.03 from 67 cases), and each watched metric, `gold_recall@20` included, is printed with the baseline interval, delta, tolerance, and pass or REGRESSION, in the compare output and in a gated-families table in the job summary.
  - **Freeze.** `scripts/commit-derived-baselines.py` replaces the documented freeze snippet: `freeze` refuses unless the source run is a completed, successful `commit-derived-bench` run, validates all eight baselines before writing, and writes them through temporary files and renames with rollback, so either every baseline changes or none does; `check` validates the checked-in baselines. `freeze --accept-regression "<reason>" --jobs-json <jobs>` freezes a failed run only when the jobs API shows that every step except `Compare against the frozen baseline` succeeded and at least one comparison failed, refuses an empty reason, and records the reason and source run id in each baseline's `provenance.accepted_regression`. The validator additionally rejects a `by_task_family` section whose family case counts plus `unassigned_cases` differ from `scored_cases`, and a family without a well-formed fingerprint, and still requires `provenance.source_commit`. No baseline is frozen by this change (#388).
- `ok retrieval-bench` reports two advisory strategies under `stream_ablations`: `fusion_pool_max` ranks the `fusion` candidate pool with `text_relevance` divided by the pool's highest lexical score, and `fusion_rank10` with `text_relevance` replaced by `(k + 1) / (k + rank)`, k = 10. Both are excluded from `benchmarks/retrieval-baseline.json` and the release thresholds, and `ok search`, `ok eval`, `ok prove` and context packs still rank with the unscaled score.
- MCP tool calls refused because of the index's state carry `data: {state, next_step}` beside their unchanged `-32000` code and message. The state is `not_indexed`, `indexing_in_progress`, `index_unavailable`, or `index_newer_than_binary` (a database `user_version` or manifest `schema_version` newer than the binary). `SqliteStore::probe_repo_index` classifies it from the index, not from the message, and `IndexRefusalState` in `open-kioku-storage` names it. Retired and unknown tool names carry no `data`. When `ok watch` withdraws the manifest after an incremental update fails, it records the reason in a new `manifest_withdrawals` table. `repo_status` and `ok --json status` then return that reason as `reason` on the not-indexed status, only while no manifest is published. Publishing a manifest, by any binary (a trigger on `manifests`), or replacing the rows removes it; the field is absent otherwise. `ok status` and `ok doctor` print that reason beside the not-indexed sentence, as `repo_status` returns it. The probe opens the index without running the schema statements, so asking whether an index can be served adds no table, index or trigger to it and takes no write transaction; and an index that cannot be opened or read while `ok index` or `ok watch` holds the lock is `indexing_in_progress`, not `index_unavailable`, so MCP no longer tells an agent to rebuild while `ok doctor` says wait. (#452)
- New public items: `open_kioku_storage::require_search_query` and `BLANK_SEARCH_QUERY_MESSAGE`, `open_kioku_storage::generations::IndexRefusalState` and `NotIndexedStatus::reason`, `open_kioku_graph::resolve_graph_node`, `open_kioku_plan::PlanOrigin` and `ContractBuilder::from_plan_with_origin`, and `SqliteStore::probe_repo_index` (with `IndexOpenRefusal`), `repo_not_indexed_status`, `not_indexed_status` and `manifest_withdrawal`.
- `ok semantic index` and `ok semantic rebuild` print embedding progress on stderr, when embedding starts, at most every two seconds, and when it finishes: targets embedded out of those the embedding cache could not reuse, the reused count, and elapsed time (`semantic[index] 512/4096 targets embedded (12.5%), reused=380/4476, elapsed=41.3s`). Standard output, `--json` included, is unchanged. An interactive terminal gets one redrawn line; redirected stderr, `NO_COLOR` set to a non-empty value, and `TERM=dumb` each get whole lines instead. `open-kioku-semantic` adds `SemanticIndexProgress` and `SemanticIndexManager::index_with_progress` / `rebuild_with_progress`. `open-kioku-embeddings` adds `EmbeddingProvider::embed_document_batch_with_progress`, a provided method that embeds `batch_size` windows in order by default; the local neural provider overrides it to report after each of its own length-sorted batches, so its batching is unchanged, while an implementation that relies on the default and would otherwise embed the whole input in one call sees one call per window. `docs/vector-index.md` records the ANN scale posture against all four measured series: `exact-flat` stays the default; persistent ANN traversing a graph of 300,000 vectors or more reports a best-effort-recall caveat instead of being rerouted; and from 100,000 up to 300,000 it reports that recall for the profile is unmeasured there rather than answering without a caveat. Both bands key on the size of the graph being traversed, not on the candidates left after a path or allowlist filter, because a filtered ANN search still walks the whole graph — the caveat names both counts (#372).
- `ok bench self --commits N` benchmarks context retrieval against the repository's own recent history (`docs/retrieval-benchmark.md`, "Benchmarking a repository against its own history"). It selects the last `N` non-merge commits that modify one to five programming-language files, applying the commit-derived extractor's subject rules (reimplemented in Rust, differences listed in the docs). For each commit it checks out the parent in a temporary git worktree, indexes that checkout inside the temporary directory, builds the context pack for the commit subject, and records the rank of every file the commit modified. It reports R@5, R@20, and MRR in aggregate and per routed task family, with case counts and each base's index coverage. Paths are redacted to `ok prove`'s path shapes and commit ids, subjects, and error messages are withheld unless `--reveal-paths` is given; `--json` prints the report. The repository's `.ok/` is never opened: each base is indexed with `security.deny_network` forced on and SCIP, semantic search, and the runtime integration off, and git runs with transports and repository hooks disabled. The checkout is removed after each case, on error, and on SIGINT or SIGTERM (exit 130), through `git worktree remove` or, when its directory is already gone, by deleting only its own administrative entry; `git worktree prune` is never run, so other stale registrations are left in place. Metrics are withheld, and the command exits non-zero, unless at least `--min-cases` cases scored (10 by default) and at least half the selected commits scored, so an indexing change that makes most cases unanswerable cannot raise the score over the few that remain; `metrics_coverage_adjusted` scores each unanswerable case as a miss beside it, and both sets carry `gold_recall@20` and the `cases` they were computed over. `--rev` pins the base so two builds can be scored on the same cases, and because the walk otherwise starts at `HEAD` — which makes every qualifying commit on the working branch a case, a benchmark's own commit usually missing on its own subject — the report records `selection.walked_repository_head` and carries a caveat when it happened, each case carries a `case_id` (the commit id, or a digest of it under redaction) to join two reports by identity rather than by row position, and the report records repository, base and case-set digests plus the effective `[ranking]` and `[history]` settings. A subject whose conventional-commit scope names a segment of a modified path is skipped, because `path_matches_scope` rewards it, and every case reports whether that boost fired on a modified file; that rule has its own `scope_names_modified_path` skip count, apart from the extractor's `path_in_subject`, so the corpus it costs on a repository of scoped subjects is visible. Sample resolution, build identity and the remaining caveats are built per run into the artifact, and the text output prints only the decimals the sample resolves. `crates/open-kioku-cli/tests/bench_self.rs` runs it on a scripted repository of 48 source files, five of which share a query's vocabulary without being what the commit changed, so that a pack holds competitors and an assertion about rank can fail at all; it pins the position retrieval gives the modified file, and checks that two runs report identically, that nothing under `.ok/` changed in contents or modification time, that an unrelated stale worktree registration survives, that no worktree or temporary directory remains, that a run below the floor publishes no metrics and says why, and that `--rev` keeps case identity across selections (#385).

### Changed

- .NET build output and restored packages are recognised on the evidence MSBuild and NuGet write, like other ecosystems' (#684). A `bin/` beside a `.csproj`, `.fsproj` or `.vbproj` is pruned as `msbuild_output` once the build has written into it (`*.dll`, `*.pdb`, `*.exe`, `*.deps.json`, or `Debug/`/`Release/`), and an `obj/` once it holds restore output (`project.assets.json`, with or without a project beside it) or, beside a project file, a `Debug/`/`Release/` directory; each is named in coverage with its tracked file count. A `bin/` of scripts beside a project file the build has not written into is walked, and a `.sln` alone does not count. A script committed into a `bin/` the build shares is counted as missing source, named in the coverage caveat and given no forbidden rule, never hidden as output. A coverage gap prices each restored NuGet package directory as installed dependencies, in the `packages.config` layout (`packages/<Id>.<Version>/` holding its `.nupkg` or `<Id>.nuspec`) or a global packages folder inside the repository (`<id>/<version>/` holding `.nupkg.metadata`); the rest of `packages/`, an authored `.nuspec` packing first-party code, and a JavaScript workspace's packages stay first-party. Files named as .NET tools write them (`*.g.cs`, `*.g.i.cs`, `*.Designer.cs`, and `*.AssemblyInfo.cs` under `obj/`) are flagged generated once C# files are indexed. A strong-name key pair (`*.snk`) and a user-secrets store (`UserSecrets/<id>/secrets.json`) follow the secret-path policy; a `secrets.json` anywhere else is indexed with its values redacted, as before.
- `ok doctor`, `ok status` and `ok index` print each pruned directory's git-tracked source count beside its name when it is non-zero (`dist/ (30 tracked source files)`) instead of "contents not counted", and list directories holding committed files ahead of empty ones, so the three named are the ones that matter. A secret-like pruned directory stays unnamed (#661).
- MCP `dependency_path` and `ok path` return, of equally short routes, the one whose weakest hop contributes most to it, with containment after a relationship hop capped as `route_authority` caps it (#660). They returned the first shortest route found, trying each node's stronger hops first, so from a file that imports another file and also defines a symbol that calls into it, the route to that file's symbol could run through the import and the capped `DEFINES` and read `corroborating` while an equally short authoritative route through the file's own symbol existed. Length still comes first: a longer route is never returned for being stronger, and of routes equal in length and strength the first one found is returned. That is the route the plain walk returned wherever no later route to a node is stronger than the first one found to it; where a stronger one replaced an earlier route, ties among the rest are deterministic but may resolve differently than before. The SQLite and in-memory stores share the walk. `ok path` without `--json` prints the same caveat text as MCP.
- `edge_authority` on MCP `dependency_path`, `get_references` and `explain_flow`, and the authority `ok path` prints after each hop, report parsed `CONTAINS` and `DEFINES` edges as `authoritative`; they read `heuristic`, the class of a symbol-registry name match, because containment carries no relationship proof (#651). Containment from the regex fallback stays `heuristic`, and every relationship edge reports what it did. What impact, plan, test selection and verify accept as proven is unchanged: they read relationship authority, not this report.
- MCP `dependency_path` with `to` returns `route_authority`, the weakest contribution of the route's hops, in which a `CONTAINS` or `DEFINES` hop after a relationship hop counts at most `corroborating`; a capped hop is listed in `hop_route_authority` and explained in `caveats` (#651). `edge_authority` still reports what each edge establishes on its own. `ok path` without `--json` now always prints the hops with their authority and the route's, followed by the same caveat when it applies; it printed the raw edge JSON, with no authority, whenever that was under 4 KB. `ok --json path` printed the edge list MCP returns under `edges`, without the authority; since #660 it prints the whole route report (see Breaking).
- Workflow benchmark: the `plan-engine` and `ingest-history` cases in `benchmarks/workflow-cases.json` expected impact files upstream of the change (`open-kioku-context` and `open-kioku-git`, which the changed crates depend on). They now expect real dependents that call the changed API: `ok plan`/`ok preflight` and the MCP plan tools for `plan-engine`, and `ok index` and `ok watch` for `ingest-history`. Suite impact recall moves from 0.900 to 0.925 because `ok index`'s command file was already in the `ingest-history` impact list; `plan-engine` finds neither of its dependents and stays at 0.0. Reasons are recorded in `docs/workflow-benchmarks.md` (#557).
- Commit-derived baselines re-frozen, per-family sections included, from one hosted `commit-derived-bench` matrix run of `bd5d06ab` (run 36130905144); a second run of the same commit (36130915229) was identical in every aggregate and per-family score and every membership fingerprint (timings aside), on both splits of all four corpora. Nineteen routed families of 34 or more cases are now gated at `max(0.03, 2/n)`; the other 27 family entries are insufficient and informational. Holdout R@5 / R@20 / MRR now read Java 0.566 / 0.708 / 0.516 (was 0.566 / 0.699 / 0.504), Go 0.690 / 0.821 / 0.539 (was 0.679 / 0.809 / 0.535), TypeScript 0.826 / 0.880 / 0.683 (was 0.825 / 0.874 / 0.658), Python 0.663 / 0.754 / 0.543 (was 0.663 / 0.759 / 0.545). Lower than the 2026-09-08 freeze, all inside the 0.03 slack and recorded rather than hidden (four-decimal baseline values): Python holdout R@1 0.4472 → 0.4422, R@20 0.7588 → 0.7538, MRR 0.5454 → 0.5426; Python dev R@20 0.7251 → 0.7208; Java dev R@10 0.6718 → 0.6679, R@20 0.7595 → 0.7519. The yield fields are not compared: the previous freeze scored them before the abstention denominator change. TypeScript has 555 cases where it had 551 (167 holdout, 388 dev), so its numbers are over a slightly different case set. Tables in README, `docs/retrieval-benchmark.md` and the site match the frozen files (#388).
- `ok context`, `ok plan`, `build_context_pack` and `plan_change` read the index coverage record and report its gaps. A gap is a programming language whose index coverage changes what an absence means: git ignore rules excluded at least 20 of its files and more than it has considered, policy left no programming-language source to consider, or it is under 98% indexed by the `ok doctor` rule. Git-ignored directories are walked and each file counted, so a git-ignored `venv/` or `out/` is a gap; name-pruned directories (`.venv`, `build`, `dist`, `node_modules`, `target`) are not. Each gap adds a caveat (`index coverage: 25 of 27 rust source files (92.6%) are not indexed (git-ignore); an absence among them is not evidence`), a zero-weight `index_coverage` component with `coverage:<language>:<cause>` evidence ids, and a `coverage` negative evidence item that is reported, not counted. A gap alone moves no label: coverage caveats are exempt from the 0.94 caveat cap. Labels move only beside a majority gap (at least half of the language's judged files missing) and a symptom it could explain. An unmatched named task identifier or an empty primary context moves the label to `Low` (0.50 cap) with the blocker `the task may name code in source the index excluded: …`; the anchor next probe no longer says the name does not exist in this repository, and a plan adds the risk reason `low confidence: named task anchor(s) … may be defined in source the index excluded: …`. A primary context in the excluded language stops below `High` (0.74 cap) with the blocker `the selected context is in a language the index mostly excluded: …`, whether or not the task named an identifier the context matched: the label follows what the index holds, not how the task was phrased, so `Exact` is unreachable for a selection in a language the index read a minority of. A gap in a language the selection does not use, or below the majority share, is reported and changes no score, as does every repository without a gap. An index that published no coverage record at all (a cross-project index, an imported snapshot, a manifest predating coverage recording, or one that cannot be read) is no longer indistinguishable from full coverage: it carries the caveat `index coverage is unrecorded: …`, its own `coverage` negative evidence item, and the 0.94 caveat cap. A coverage record that cannot be read at all is reported separately as `index coverage could not be read from the manifest: …`, because that is a claim about the read rather than about the index. `ok index`, `ok status` and `ok status --markdown` print a `coverage gap:` line beside the coverage summary, whose ratio is computed over considered files and so reads near-100% in exactly the git-ignore case a gap describes. `ok preflight` and MCP `plan_change` with `detail: "preflight"` now return `start_with_caution` instead of `safe_to_start` when a majority coverage gap matches the selected context's language, joining the existing evidence-completeness triggers (unresolved imports, ambiguous edges); the general `Medium` to `safe_to_start` mapping is unchanged. The `repo_status` tool description names `coverage_gaps`, and a new golden, `repo_status_coverage_gaps.json`, pins the field's serialized shape.
- MCP `search_code` answers through the same ranked search as `ok search` in every mode (`open_kioku_context::search::ranked_search`), so a query returns the same ordered paths and line ranges on both surfaces, and a `search_code` page at `offset` is a slice of `ok search`'s list (#448). Previously `mode: code` returned the search index's order, `mode: semantic` the vector index's, and `mode: hybrid` sorted BM25 and similarity scores together without weights. Response schemas are unchanged: `search_code` declares its `outputSchema` as an open object (`additionalProperties: true`) that names no result fields, so neither `score` nor the `score_breakdown` signal names are part of it, and under `STABILITY.md` result order is behaviour, not schema. For `search_code` callers:
  - **Order and scores.** `code`, `semantic` and `hybrid` results are reranked with the repository's `[ranking]` weights, which `ok mcp serve` reads from `ok.toml` when it starts. `score` is the fused score, and `score_breakdown` carries the ranking signals (`text_relevance`, `boundary_fit`, …) in place of the index's `bm25_relevance` and `query_variant_boost` parts, as `ok search` already reported. `mode: graph` keeps the index's order on both surfaces.
  - **One result per path.** A file matched in several chunks takes one slot, its best-ranked chunk. In `hybrid` that result also carries the path's semantic evidence.
  - **Depth.** Each source is asked for `4 × (offset + limit)` candidates, clamped to 100..500, where it was asked for `offset + limit + 1` up to 500, so no page is fetched from a shallower pool than before. A file counts once, so a deep page can hold fewer results than the same page of the raw index did when files match in several chunks. When a source filled its window and the page ends the results, the response sets `truncated` with a warning and leaves `has_more` false. `limit: 0` still returns an empty page with `has_more`.
  - **Co-change candidates.** Files that local git history records as co-changing with a result can appear, as they do in `ok search`. Such a result matched no search term, and says so in `match_reason`; the `tools/list` description of `search_code` now says so too, along with the reranking and the one-result-per-file rule.
  - **A forgotten `query` reads as one.** `search_code` reports a `query` that is absent, or that holds something other than a string, as ``invalid input: missing required string argument `query` ``, the words `get_definition` and `retrieve_context` use; it read as a blank query before. A `query` that is present and blank keeps the shared `search requires a non-empty query` message. Both are `-32602`.
  - `ok search` code and hybrid output is unchanged. `ok search --semantic` now asks the vector index for the same candidate depth as the other modes, where it asked for exactly `--limit` hits, so its order can change. `ok search --limit` above 50 ranks up to `4 × limit` candidates (at most 500) where it ranked 200, so results past the first 50 can differ. `ok search --limit 0` still prints every unique path in the candidate pool, now also with `--hybrid`, which printed nothing, and with `--kind graph`, up to 500 documents. A missing graph index is reported as ``index error: graph search index is missing; run `ok index .` first`` on both surfaces: `search_code` returned that sentence without the `index error: ` prefix before, at the same `-32000` code.
- Import-bound call, receiver, declared-type and inheritance resolution in every language looks a name up through the scopes enclosing the use, nearest first: the nearest scope that imports the name decides, an explicit import shadows a glob in the same scope, and an import in a nearer scope shadows one further out. An import inside one function or `mod tests` block no longer binds a call or type use elsewhere in the file, and a module-level call in the same scope as its import can now bind through it. When the deciding import is unresolved, or a glob in a nearer scope could supply the name, the import rule produces no candidate and resolution falls back to the same-file and qualified-name rules. In Rust a `mod` block does not see the imports of the module around it, except through `use super::*;` (or `use super::super::*;` within the same file), which continues the lookup in the parent module when no other glob in the block could supply the name. In Python an `if`, `try`, `with` or loop body is not a scope for this lookup, so an import made in one (such as under `if TYPE_CHECKING:`) binds in the enclosing function, class or module, and the two imports of a `try:`/`except ImportError:` pair both stay candidates; a method body does not see imports made in its class body (#453).
- The built-in secret-path rule blocks only key material and environment entries (`.env`, `.env.*`, `.aws`, `.ssh`, `id_rsa*`, `id_ed25519*`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `*.jks`, `*.keystore`), for every file. Data, config, and prose files whose path merely names a secret (`config/secrets.yaml`, `credentials.json`, `docs/SECRETS.md`, `signing_key.txt`) were skipped as `secret_policy`; they are now indexed with their secret-like values redacted (see the redaction entry under Added), so their keys are searchable. On the next `ok index` such files move from `secret_policy` skips to indexed files in coverage and can raise `quality.redacted_files`. `[paths] deny` still excludes any path, and the default configuration still denies `**/secrets/**`. The semantic corpus applies the same rule (`open_kioku_core::is_secret_like_path`) instead of excluding every path containing `secret` or `.env`, so it embeds the files the lexical index holds, from the same redacted chunks (#379).
- An `ok watch` event over an index whose analysis semantics are compatible but whose `IndexManifest.schema_version` is older than 3 re-indexes the repository in full: `partial_index_supported` requires the stored and current versions to match.
