- A full `ok index` over an existing index refuses reads with `indexing in progress` from the row transaction until the manifest is published, covering the history, graph, and Tantivy stages; it previously served the new rows over the previous graph once the row transaction committed. Building the next index in a staging generation under `.ok/generations/` and publishing it with the atomic `active` pointer (`crates/open-kioku-storage/src/generations.rs`) is the follow-up that keeps the previous index readable during a rebuild.
- `ok doctor`'s coverage check warns when git ignore rules (`.gitignore`, `.git/info/exclude`, or `core.excludesFile`) exclude at least 20 files of a programming language and more than that language's considered files (`[ok] coverage 12 of 12 programming-language files indexed (100.0%) ... 640 excluded by policy` is now `[warn]`, naming the language and the git ignore rules); the next step says to remove the rule, or to list the paths under `[index] exclude` if the exclusion is intended, which is checked first and stops the warning. `hidden`, `vendor`, `fast_mode`, `denied`, `[index] exclude` and `.okignore` exclusions stay informational, and with the default `[security] allow_hidden_files = false` a git-ignored worktree under a hidden directory still counts as `hidden`. The rule reads the new `IndexCoverage.policy_excluded_by_language` (source counts per language, in `ok --json status` and `repo_status`); a manifest written before it existed never warns until the next `ok index`.
- Coverage's denominator is the files the index would consider under the current policy: discovered files minus those skipped as `hidden`, `ignored`, `denied`, `secret_policy`, `vendor`, `generated`, `fast_mode`, or `symlink_policy`. Those policy exclusions are reported beside the ratio — in `ok index`, `ok doctor`, `ok status`, and `repo_status` — with counts by reason, the top directories (`IndexCoverage.policy_excluded_dirs`), and the setting that governs the largest share (`policy_excluded_by_source`; `hidden` names `[security] allow_hidden_files`, `ignored` names `.gitignore`, `.okignore`, or `[index] exclude`). The under-98% warning, the per-language `!` marker, and the missing-file rule are judged on that basis; the doctor's coverage next step names `[index] max_file_size` when `too-large` dominates the remaining omissions and no longer points at `[index] exclude` or `.okignore` for a `hidden` skip. Existing manifests read the same way without re-indexing (`discovered` and `skipped` are unchanged; the two new maps are empty until the next `ok index`). On this repository `ok doctor` went from `[warn] coverage 241 of 1,690 programming-language files indexed (14.3%)` to `[ok] coverage 241 of 241 programming-language files indexed (100.0%); 440 of 440 recognised files indexed (100.0%) overall; 3,394 excluded by policy (3,386 hidden, 8 ignored; 3,344 under .claude/, 23 under .github/, 6 under .playwright-cli/; `[security] allow_hidden_files` governs the largest share)`, the hidden files being git-ignored agent worktrees.
- The corpus benchmark workflows, `commit-derived-bench.yml` and `semantic-experiment.yml`, now keep corpus identity out of public logs and artifacts:
  - **Secrets.** Each corpus's repository URL, base commit, and indexed subtree list is read from the `BENCH_<CODE>_URL`, `BENCH_<CODE>_BASE`, and (for a corpus indexed as subtrees) `BENCH_<CODE>_PATHS` repository secrets. The subtree list is no longer checked in: it leaves the workflow matrix, and `provenance.path_prefixes` in the baselines becomes `provenance.path_prefix_count`.
  - **Masking.** `scripts/mask-corpus-identity.sh` normalises these values and masks every form of them a log can still show.
  - **Clone and index output.** The clone and its remote are removed once cases are derived. `ok index` and `ok status` write to files, `scripts/extract-ok-json.py` reads the document past any log lines, and the log gets one counts-only line.
  - **Manual runs.** They no longer accept a repository URL or base commit. A `corpus` choice (`all` by default, which is what the schedule runs) limits a trial run to one corpus. Every input is validated, and none reach a `run:` script as expression text.
  - **Case derivation.** `scripts/commit-derived-cases.py --quiet` prints counts only and withholds failure details.
  - **Artifacts.** Uploads carry aggregate results only. `scripts/reduce-benchmark-report.py` keeps allowlisted fields of allowlisted shapes after the baseline comparison, and `cases.tsv` is not uploaded. The documented baseline freeze reads `cases_scored` from the reduced reports.
- The parser semantics version is `tier1-parser-semantics-v9`, so an index built by an earlier release reports `analysis_semantics_status: RebuildRequired`. Until it is rebuilt, MCP `plan_change`, `impact_analysis`, `get_references`, `dependency_path` and `query_evidence_graph`, `ok graph query` and `ok search --kind graph` answer "authoritative relationship evidence unavailable", and `ok watch` fails on every change event. Next step: `ok index <repo>`.
- Each evidence line of a context-pack primary result is its own record, `search:<path>:<range>:<index>`; a result ref that is not one of its lines' ids resolves to no record. Pack and plan evidence are deduplicated by id, `validation_plan.evidence` holds only the records its tests cite, and evidence density counts one fact per result path and range.
- `impact_analysis`, `ok impact`, context packs and plans list one direct impact per path and edge kind (exact reference, co-change, runtime, service boundary, lexical), up to 25 files; an entry carries the evidence lines of up to four further chunks of its path and one line naming the ranges of the rest. A plan's caution files are its structural impacts and at most eight lexical impact paths, direct impacts before indirect ones; the Markdown and prompt-text context renderings list each supporting path once. Impact risk counts these entries, so a file matched in several chunks raises it once per edge kind rather than once per chunk: on the demo repository `ok impact --file src/auth.rs` reports risk `low` (0.21) where it reported `medium` (0.31).
- The edge filter fields of `query_evidence_graph` and `ok graph query` are `evidence_source` and `evidence_source_type`, not `source` and `source_type`. In Cypher an edge's source is the node it leaves, so `WHERE c.source = 'auth'` read as "edges leaving `auth`" while it filtered on the pass that recorded the evidence, and returned no rows and no caveat. `source` and `source_type` on any variable are now a `parse_error` that says an edge's source node is the node variable on its left (filter its `label` or `id`), its type is written in the pattern (`(a:Function)`), and the evidence fields are `evidence_source` and `evidence_source_type`. Edge filters were added in this release cycle and have not appeared in a release, so no released query changes: in 4.0.0 `source` and `source_type` parsed only on node variables and matched no row. The evidence schema's `syntax`, its edge-evidence example and its `evidence_field_on_a_node` alternative use the new names.

### Fixed

- Helpers, fixtures and lifecycle hooks in test files are no longer validation (#495). Every callable in a test-path file used to be a test target that planning and validation availability accepted by provenance alone, so `setUp`, `beforeEach`, `TestMain`, `makeClient`, `withTempRepo`, a `@pytest.fixture` and every exported function of a `testutil/` package were plannable tests, and a repository of helpers with no runnable test scored `validation_availability` 1.0. A test-file callable is now a test only when it matches its runner's default discovery rule: a Rust test attribute (`#[test]`, `#[tokio::test]`, `#[rstest]`, `#[proptest]`, `#[pg_test]` and an explicit list of others, through `cfg_attr` too, but not a `#[template]` or `#[fixture]`); a JUnit or TestNG test annotation (so `@Test void shouldRoundHalfUp()` stays a test), a JUnit 3 `public void test*()` of a class that extends another, outside files written for JUnit 4 or 5, or a public method of a class TestNG annotates `@Test`; a Python `test*` function in a `test*.py` or `*_test.py` module, at module level or in a `Test*` class without `__init__` or a class with a base (so `unittest` `test_*` methods stay tests, inherited bases included); a Go `TestX`, `FuzzX` or output `ExampleX` in a `_test.go` file; or a JavaScript or TypeScript registration call. Everything else is kept as test code, which impact still leaves out of a file's search terms, but `ok plan` no longer records it as validation, `ok verify` no longer expects it, and the validation stream no longer votes for it. Runner configuration is not read, so surfaces say such a callable matched no default discovery rule rather than that no runner executes it, and never drop it silently: a plan whose nearby targets include them says `N test-file callable(s) near this change matched no default runner discovery rule (runner configuration is not read)` in its risk reasons and next step instead of `No indexed tests were found`, and the context pack's validation negative evidence carries the same clause; callables whose names share a word with the task count as near, and a plan that selects nothing with none near still names the callables the index withheld instead of claiming no tests exist. Under a pytest configuration (`pytest.ini`, `.pytest.ini`, `pyproject.toml` `[tool.pytest]` or `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, `tox.ini`, `setup.cfg`) that sets `python_files`, `python_classes` or `python_functions`, Python test files fall back to counting every callable as a test, with a `test_discovery` quality note. A repository holding nothing but such callables reports validation unavailable with the caveat `every indexed test target is a test-file callable matching no default runner discovery rule (runner configuration is not read)` and `validation_availability` 0.2.
- `ok verify --diff`, `ok contract verify --diff` and MCP `verify_change` with `diff` read a supplied diff under every path-prefix style git writes (#675). Before, the `---`/`+++` paths had `a/` or `b/` stripped one by one whatever the diff's style. Under `diff.mnemonicPrefix` (`c/`, `i/`, `w/`, `o/`) that recorded paths such as `w/src/lib.rs` that were never changed and missed the real ones, so a forbidden or out-of-boundary edit could pass; under `diff.noprefix` a real `a/` or `b/` directory was stripped. Each `diff --git` entry's style is now read from its header, checked against its `rename`/`copy` and `---`/`+++` lines, and applied to all of them: the default `a/`/`b/`, none, or a mnemonic pair, either way round, since `-R` swaps them. For diffs git wrote under each style, with additions, deletions, renames, binary files, mode changes, names holding spaces and directories named `a/` and `w/`, `changed_files` equals `git diff --name-only --no-renames`. An entry whose paths fit no style or more than one, such as one written with a custom `diff.srcPrefix`, fails verification as invalid input (exit 2, JSON-RPC `-32602`) naming the entry's position and header line, not a path. Diffs `ok` runs itself pin `a/` and `b/` and are read byte for byte as before. `open_kioku_git::unified_diff::with_default_prefixes` and `UnreadableDiffEntry` are new public items.
- `ok index` no longer fails on a repository with a checked-out submodule (#677). Discovery sent the submodule's files to `git check-ignore`, which rejects the whole batch when one path lies inside a submodule ("Pathspec ... is in submodule"). The submodule is now pruned as another repository: its files are not indexed, and it is named in coverage and `skipped_paths` like any pruned directory, except at a secret-like path, where it is only counted. A submodule nested inside it or under `node_modules` is cut with the outer directory; an uninitialised one has no `.git` and is walked. A directory whose `.git` is stray, because this repository tracks files under it, is still pruned, but its tracked source counts as missing (`PruneReason::counts_tracked_source` is true for `Submodule`), a plan sets no rule for it, and `ok doctor` says to remove the `.git`. A folder of clones that is not itself a repository is still walked into each clone. `ok watch` ignores edits inside a submodule, and a plan forbids `<submodule>/**`, the files under it. A bump edits only the gitlink, which that rule does not cover: `ok verify` reports it as `out_of_boundary`, which `--evidence-ref` admits. `open_kioku_patch::forbidden_rule_matches` is new: a forbidden rule citing `coverage:pruned:<dir>` covers the files under `<dir>`, not `<dir>` itself. A folder of clones that is not a repository but sits under one higher up has each clone pruned. `ok snapshot import` of an artifact that served files under what is now a submodule no longer fails the same way: when `git check-ignore` fails, paths under the gitlinks Git records are dropped from the question and the rest are asked again. Such files are pruned where the submodule is checked out; where it is not initialised, they are served like any file the checkout does not hold.
- `ok impact --since` and MCP `impact_analysis` with `since` account for every path `git diff --name-status` names (#671). Git writes some entries with no `---`/`+++` lines: an empty file added or deleted, a binary file added, modified or deleted, and a mode change. Their paths were read from nowhere, so they were missing from `changed_files`, the reports and `removed_paths_not_indexed`, and the accounting held only against the parsed list; on a 336-entry diff of this repository, 8 paths were absent. They are now read from the `diff --git` line, with git's C-style quoting decoded, the `a/`/`b/` prefixes dropped only when both sides carry them (so a `--no-prefix` path that starts with `a/` keeps it), and a path holding ` b/` split where both halves name the same path; `new file mode`, `deleted file mode`, `rename`/`copy` and `similarity index` lines give the kind of change. A binary change has no line ranges, so its whole file is the change: its report touches every symbol the index holds for it and its file node, and a removed binary file is reported or listed as any removed file is. An entry with no hunks that is not binary touches no line, and its report says so where that could change the answer. `ok plan`, `ok preflight` and MCP `plan_change` with `since` list these entries too, a binary one as `binary`. `ok verify` and MCP `verify_change` read the `diff --git` line with the same function, so a `--no-prefix` diff's binary and mode-only entries now reach the changed-file set and its boundary checks. Every `git diff` these surfaces run pins `--submodule=short`: under `diff.submodule=log` a changed submodule had no entry, and under `diff.submodule=diff` the files inside it were read as paths of this repository.
- A coverage gap names the directories its missing files came from, and a gap made of installed third-party packages no longer caps confidence the way missing first-party source does (#503). Before, a gap named only its language, so a git-ignored `site-packages` tree and a git-ignored `generated/` tree of first-party code were one `python` gap, and a selection in Python stopped at 0.74 whichever it was. The index now records policy-excluded files per language and directory, and classes as `dependencies` only the directory a package tool installs into, on evidence the tool wrote: the `lib/python*/site-packages` (or Windows `Lib/site-packages`) of a directory holding `pyvenv.cfg` or `conda-meta/` (a Python environment under any name), a `site-packages` or `dist-packages` directory holding `*.dist-info` or `*.egg-info` metadata, or a `vendor` directory holding `modules.txt` (Go) or `composer/installed.json` (Composer). A marker never classes the tree around it: a git-ignored `generated/` beside a `pyvenv.cfg` that `python -m venv` wrote into a first-party service directory, and the environment's own `bin/`, stay source. Anything else is `unclassified` and priced as first-party source. The 0.50 and 0.74 caps, the `index_coverage_selected_language` signal that withholds `safe_to_start`, and a plan's coverage risk reason now apply when most of the language's possibly-first-party files are missing, counted without the installed dependencies. A gap of dependencies alone is still reported: its caveat names each directory, class and evidence, and the `index_coverage` component and `coverage` negative evidence item carry it as before. `repo_status` and `ok --json status` report `dependency_files` and `excluded_dirs` on each gap, and `coverage.policy_excluded_dirs_by_language`. An index written before this has no per-language directories and is priced as before until `ok index` runs again.
- `ok impact` and MCP `impact_analysis` read every proven edge of a hub symbol when the store's counts show more than the 40 edges one read takes (#665). Before, those reads stopped at 40 edges, and the proven edges past them were only counted as unread. On this repository's largest file at commit 6fcaae00, eight such reads left 228 proven edges unread. A read is now widened to the counted proven edges, up to 400 edges in one read and 2,000 extra edges across one report. The budget is spent in the order the nodes are read: the changed file's own node, then its symbols, most important first. Measured on this repository at commit 6fcaae00, the largest window held 121 proven edges, and all windows together held 735 proven edges past their first 40, so no report there met either bound. `relationship_impact_reads.windows_widened` counts the widened reads. `windows_at_limit`, `windows_cutting_proven` and `proven_edges_unread` count what a read left out past its widened window. The caveat names both limits. With release builds of that commit and of this change, on one macOS workstation, `ok impact --file crates/open-kioku-core/src/lib.rs` now reads 8 widened windows. `proven_edges_unread` falls from 228 to 0, and `proven_impact_omitted` rises from 991 to 1,197 as the extra edges are counted. Across five runs of each build, the file took 7.1 to 10.2 s with this change and 7.4 to 15.1 s before. The spread is machine load, and no change in latency could be measured. On `crates/open-kioku-storage-sqlite/src/lib.rs`, 4 widened windows take `proven_edges_unread` from 81 to 0, at 3.8 to 4.0 s with this change and 4.0 to 7.8 s before.
- `ok impact --since` and MCP `impact_analysis` with `since` report the files a diff deletes (#668). Before, a deleted file got no report and was not counted in `impact_reports_omitted`, though its dependents are the ones most certain to break. While the index still holds such a file, it is reported from the dependents the index last held for it, with the file itself and every symbol it defined touched. Its proven dependents, including the files that import it by path, are then kept up to the bound of 200, and its first caveat and risk reason say the file is gone. It ranks among the changed paths by those dependents, ahead of a path with as many proven ones. When the index does not hold the file (it was rebuilt since the change, or never indexed the path, as with a secret-like path), nothing can be read for it. It is then listed in the new `removed_paths_not_indexed` field with a caveat of its own, apart from `impact_reports_omitted`, so the reports, the omitted paths and those paths add up to every changed path. A rename counts as removing its previous path and changing its new one, so both are changed paths. Two files that swap names remove neither path. `ok impact --file F --since R` and MCP `path` with `since` report a removed `F` the same way. The text output lists the removed paths that are not indexed. The previous path of a rename that the index never held, which had a report with no dependents, is now listed there instead.
- `ok path`, `ok --json path` and MCP `dependency_path` no longer answer a search cut short by the 12-hop limit with a bare empty route, which read the same as "not connected" (#666). When no route is found and the search stopped at the limit with nodes it had reached still unexpanded, the report carries `stopped_at_hop_limit: 12` and a caveat naming the limit, and `ok path` prints `No dependency path found within 12 hops.` with it. An empty route without them means the search read every node `from` reaches without meeting `to`: no route of any length over the edges the walk follows, which are forward edges other than `DERIVED_FROM`, `SIMILAR_TO` and `SEMANTICALLY_RELATED`. The walk already knew which of the two ended it, so the check reads nothing more. Routes that are found are unchanged.
- `ok snapshot import` applies the importing repository's pruning, `[index] keep_dirs` included, and no longer serves files the exporter kept that `ok index` here would never reach (#661). `ok.toml` is not committed, so an exporter that kept `build/` handed its files to every importer, the import reported nothing removed, and a later edit to such a file never showed as a change, since the freshness count leaves pruned paths out. Each is now removed with its rows, counted as `pruned` under "Removed by local index policy", and recorded in coverage as discovery here would record it.
- The remaining name-based build-directory rules follow discovery's evidence (#661). The semantic corpus no longer drops every path containing `/target/`, which left a Java `com.acme.target` package and a Rust `src/target/` module out of semantic search while the lexical index held them. The project model and the import resolver's manifest walks skip what discovery prunes instead of every `target`: a workspace member in a directory named `target` is a crate, so its `crate::` paths prove `CALLS` edges, and a `package.json` copied into a bundle's `dist/` is no longer read. `ok doctor`'s language sampling no longer counts build output or skips a walked `target` package.
- `ok index` no longer stalls on a Rust crate whose modules glob each other (`use crate::frame::*;` in one module, `use crate::util::*;` in another, and so on) (#659). Following a path through `use` re-exports kept only lookups that met no `use` cycle and stayed within the re-export hop limit. In such a crate every lookup meets one, so each walked every path through the globs to the limit, which grows exponentially with the number of globs: a video encoder crate of about 110 Rust files ran for more than 15 minutes and now indexes in about 12 seconds. A lookup cut short by a cycle or the limit is now kept as well, for any later lookup at the same depth that would find the same lookups under way and the same ones settled, and so take the same path. The globs of a module are no longer read once a named `use` or a top-level macro shadows them, or past one that leaves the name unsettled, since nothing they bring in is read then. Recorded facts are unchanged: graph and analysis facts are identical on this repository and 13 third-party crates, and no semantics version changes.
- `ok impact` and MCP `impact_analysis` read the dependents of a changed file's symbols by importance, not the first 16 in file order, and say what they left out in numbers (#656). They first count each symbol's inbound impact edges, and how many are proven, in one query, then read only the symbols that have any, at most 256: those a change touches, then those with a proven dependent, then public ones, then those with the most inbound edges. A change deep in a large file used to lose its dependents, proven ones included, and the truncation caveat fired for nearly every large file whether or not anything was skipped.
  - `relationship_impact_reads` reports symbols read and in all, unread symbols with dependents, edges and proven edges left unread, and windows that held more edges than they read (each read now asks for one past its window, so an exactly full one is not reported as cut) and how many of those may have cut a proven edge. `relationship_impact_caveats` now speaks only when something that matters was left unread.
  - `proven_impact` is cut by the rule `possible_impact` is (each other file's first entry before any file's second, the changed file's own last) and counts what it cut in `proven_impact_omitted`; it is still listed by path (#652).
  - `ok impact --symbol` and `ok impact --since`, and MCP `impact_analysis` with the new `symbol` and `since` inputs, say which symbols the change touches (by name, or by the lines each diff adds or modifies; a line touches the innermost symbol around it). Both surfaces answer through `ImpactEngine::answer`. Their dependents are read and listed first, and `proven_impact` lists every proven dependent of them up to 200. `--since` matches lines only for a file the index holds as it is on disk, and `--file` with `--since` now gives that one file's report focused on its changed lines. `ok plan` names unread proven dependents on its proven count.
  - `proven_impact_omitted_files` and `possible_impact_omitted_files` count the dependent files a cut entry is in that the list does not name at all, which an entry count cannot tell apart. `ok impact`'s text output now summarizes the relationship evidence: proven and possible counts, omitted entries and files, the symbols read, and the caveats.

  Measured with release builds of `main` (5333f458) and this change on this repository (the 55 Rust files the last 12 commits changed) and a Python repository (~4,400 indexed files; 50 files, the 20 with the most symbols and 30 others): the caveat fires on 5 of 55 files instead of 48, and on 6 of 50 instead of 25, and every remaining one reports proven edges left unread. Proven entries listed went from 594 to 1,076 and from 399 to 634. An entry `main` listed is missing only where the list is now full and the cap kept other dependents (160 and 50 entries; 103 of the 160 are in the changed file itself, which now goes last), and the dependent files `proven_impact` names, the changed file aside, went from 198 to 242 on this repository. With `ok impact --since` over the same 12 commits, all 52 proven entries `main` listed for touched symbols are kept. Impact wall time is unchanged (per-file median ratio 0.98 and 0.99, interleaved runs); the relationship reads are about 1% of it.
- A symbol-registry match made by name alone (unique project name, suffix import reachability, fuzzy) now reads as `ambiguous` in `possible_impact` (#652). The registry recorded that it was speculative only in its message; it now records it in `AnalysisFact::ambiguity`, which the graph copies onto the edge. A use several symbols of one name could answer still writes no edge.
- Similarity facts (`SIMILAR_TO`, `SEMANTICALLY_RELATED`) end at the similar symbol's node rather than at a node made from its label (#652). Similarity is not a dependency: `shortest_path` (`ok path`, MCP `dependency_path` with `to`) does not cross it and untyped neighbourhood reads leave it out, as for `DERIVED_FROM`; a typed read finds it. On this repository every `SIMILAR_TO` edge now reaches a symbol, and the retrieval baseline, every retrieval ablation and the workflow benchmark are unchanged.
- More Rust method calls through a struct field reach their method (#639). The field's type is read through a `type` alias (`type Store = Disk;`, then `self.store.save()`), and through the standard library's `Box`, `Rc` and `Arc` to the type they hold. Through a pointer, the call reaches only an inherent method of that type, and only when no method of the pointer can answer it: not a name the standard library gives the pointer (`clone`, `strong_count`, `Iterator::next` of a `Box`), not a method a trait of the repository declares, and not in a file where an import from outside the repository or a glob may bring in a trait the index does not know. A `Mutex` is not seen through. When a field's type has an inherent method and trait methods of one name, the inherent one is proven when rustc tries it no later than each of them, as it does for `&self` beside `&self`, but not for `&self` beside a trait's `self`.
- Two glob imports that bring in one name in different namespaces no longer read as a conflict (#654). After `pub use crate::a::*; pub use crate::b::*;`, where `a` holds `pub mod f` and `b` `pub fn f`, `crate::c::f()` reaches `b::f` and `crate::c::f::h()` reaches `h` in the module `a::f`, an inline block or a module file. A method call on a local built by a tuple struct's constructor (`let s = Store(1); s.save()`) or bound to a unit struct (`let s = Marker;`) reaches the struct's method, and an associated function called through an alias (`Alias::make()`, an alias of an enum or struct) reaches the aliased type's.
- A symbol-registry `CALLS` or `REFERENCES` edge now ends at the node of the symbol it resolved to, so `ok impact` and MCP `impact_analysis` report it (#475). A call the resolver cannot read, such as `settle(amount)` inside `format!`, was found by the registry, but its edge ended at an `analysis:Function:<hash>` node made from the label `ledger::settle`, beside the symbol's own node, and no symbol-keyed read reached it: the caller was in neither `proven_impact` nor `possible_impact`. The edge carries no proof and stays heuristic everywhere it is read:
  - impact lists it under `possible_impact` only, with a reason naming the pass that inferred it (`no structural proof; inferred by open-kioku-symbol-registry/<strategy>`), and no longer lists there a dependent (path and symbol) that `proven_impact` lists through any edge type;
  - it is not written where a proven or corroborated edge already joins the same two nodes with the same type, so its line never joins a proven edge's call sites, and not from a symbol to itself;
  - the context pack's graph stream gives a neighbour `corroborating` retrieval authority only through a proven or corroborated edge or parsed containment, and `heuristic` otherwise; it ranks and cuts its candidates by that authority before path, and only an evidenced graph neighbour gets the graph's priority in budget selection. Ranked by path alone, the newly reachable heuristic neighbours took the stream's first ranks and could push proven neighbours past its limit;
  - MCP `get_references` callers and callees, `dependency_path` and each `explain_flow` flow return `edge_authority`, each edge's authority by edge id; `ok path` prints it after each hop; and `explain_flow` ends a flow at its first unproven hop.

  Connected, these edges made possibilities far more numerous than proofs, so the 25-entry `possible_impact` cap now keeps dependent files apart: each other file's first entry, then each one's second, in path order, with the changed file's own symbols last, and `possible_impact_omitted` counts what it cut. That count covers what impact read, and the new `relationship_impact_caveats` says when a read stopped short: impact reads the dependents of at most 16 of a changed file's symbols and 40 inbound edges of each type per symbol, and `proven_impact` holds at most 25. `ok plan`'s confidence summary names the count or the truncation. Cut by path alone, the changed file's own symbols or one busy file filled the list and dropped whole dependent files, such as a test file paired with the change.

  Measured with release builds of `main` (28fce233) and this change, indexing the same trees: a recent snapshot of this repository and a Go service (~2,500 files). Only symbol-registry edges differ: no other edge was added, removed or changed authority, and in no 20-, 40- or 50-edge window is a proven or containment edge cut (heuristic edges are, by the new heuristic edges: 22 in 9 untyped 20-edge windows here, 45 in 21 on the Go service). `analysis:*` label nodes fell from 6,147 to 1,453 and from 9,480 to 1,327; registry `CALLS` edges from 9,950 to 3,294 and from 18,708 to 14,531, and each of the 6,656 and 4,177 removed is one a proven edge already draws; registry `REFERENCES` are unchanged in number. Over `ok impact` for 43 files changed by recent commits here and 25 sampled Go files, `proven_impact` is identical for all 68, no `possible_impact` entry `main` listed is lost, and 95 and 124 dependent files appear that `main` did not report; 177 of the 182 other-file Go entries name the changed symbol in the dependent file, and the other 5 are file-level test pairings. 40 of the 43 files here and 2 of the 25 Go files have more symbols than impact reads, which the reports now say.

  Retrieval ranking moves where the graph stream contributes. The gated retrieval baseline is unchanged, but in the stream ablations, which rank by contributing streams, graph-only retrieval finds more gold files (recall@10 0.107 to 0.333, MRR 0.24 to 0.29), both reciprocal-rank-fusion ablations reorder 7 cases (recall@10 0.987 to 1.0, MRR 0.573 to 0.583 and 0.577 to 0.587), and the routed context-pack ablation reorders 5 (MRR 0.778 to 0.790; one case's reciprocal rank 0.2 to 0.5). No case's reciprocal rank or recall falls. The workflow benchmark's metrics are unchanged; one case (`tantivy-search`) lists its top context paths and tests in a different order. The relationship corpora are unchanged in every metric (234 compact and 336 full cases, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic). The full corpus's observation digest changed in 14 cases: in 12 a heuristic duplicate of a proven edge is no longer written, and in 2 a heuristic edge's target now names the symbol instead of its label node. A compact case pins a call inside a macro to a heuristic-only edge. `GRAPH_EMISSION_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-graph-emission-v4`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- Discovery no longer drops source directories named `target`, `build`, `dist` or `.venv` (#477). It pruned every directory with one of those names, or `node_modules`, at any depth, recorded none of them by path, and coverage still read 100%, so a crate's `src/build/` module was silently missing from the index. A directory is now pruned only on evidence it is build output or installed packages: `node_modules`; a `.venv` or `venv` holding `pyvenv.cfg` or `conda-meta`; a `target` holding `CACHEDIR.TAG` or beside a `Cargo.toml`, `pom.xml`, `build.sbt`, `build.properties` or `project.clj`; a `build` or `dist` that no `mod.rs`, `<name>.rs` module file, `__init__.py`, Go package or place under `src/` declares. Each pruned directory is named in `skipped_paths` (reason `pruned`) and in `coverage.pruned` with the git-tracked source under it, at most 50 with the rest counted; git-tracked source under a `build` or `dist` pruned only because nothing declares it (`undeclared_build_dir`) counts as missing (`pruned`), and `ok doctor` warns and names the directory at any count; committed files under a directory pruned on strong evidence (a `CACHEDIR.TAG`, a build manifest beside it, such as a `dist/` bundle beside `package.json`) are listed with their count and never lower coverage. `ok doctor` names `**/build/**`, `**/dist/**` and `**/target/**` patterns an older `ok.toml` still lists. The `.gitignore`/`.okignore` walk, the Git ignore candidate walk, the snapshot freshness check and `ok watch`'s event filter apply the same rule, so `ok watch` now re-indexes on an edit under a declared `src/build/` and ignores writes to an undeclared `build/`. Rules: `docs/indexing-pipeline.md`, "Pruned directories".
- A plan's `recommended_change_boundary.allowed_symbols` now lists symbols (#648). It kept a relevant symbol when an allowed file's path equalled the symbol's file id, which is derived from the path but is not the path, so the list was empty in every plan from `ok plan` and MCP `plan_change`. Symbols are now placed through the index's file id to path mapping, and paths compare without `.` segments. The list holds the plan's `relevant_symbols` declared in an allowed file, so at most eight entries: the symbols the plan expects the edit to touch, not every symbol of those files. `ok verify` does not read the list and change contracts take their `impacted_symbols` from `relevant_symbols`, so neither verdict changes.
- Symbol listings page by an index scan again (#634). Since #621 `ok symbol find` and MCP `search_symbols` put a placed Go type alias just after the type it stands for by merging two reads of `symbols` and sorting the merge, so SQLite read every row before a page. Each row now has `symbols.list_order_key`, under a full index: the symbol's qualified name, or for a placed alias its target's qualified name, `U+0001`, then its own. `U+0001` sorts below every printable character, so the key orders a type, then its aliases, then the names that extend the type's, the order #621 defined. The key is a virtual column SQLite generates from `qualified_name` and #621's `alias_order_key`, which is still written, so a row any Open Kioku inserts has one, and #621's Open Kioku reading an index this version wrote still lists in the same order. A writer's open (`ok index`, `ok watch`, snapshot import) of an index written before the key adds it and builds its index in one transaction, rewriting no row; a read surface on such an index adds nothing and lists as before, sorting on read. A snapshot import checks the column and index definitions the artifact brings, not just their names, and replaces any it does not recognize: a plain `list_order_key`, or one generated by another expression, could list today's rows correctly and rows indexed later in another order. It still refuses an artifact whose `alias_order_key` disagrees with its row's JSON. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change through the store's own API, over the same 500,000 synthetic symbol rows (one in 100 a placed alias), on a macOS 13 laptop (Intel Core i5-1038NG7, 8 threads, 16 GB) also running other work, so compare numbers within a run. A page of 50 at offset 200,000 takes 14.8 ms, against 667 ms on `main`; the same query in plain qualified-name order, as before #621, took 15.2 ms. At offset 1,000: 0.12 ms against 3.1 ms. The full listing of the 500,000 rows is identical to `main`'s, whether the index was written by this change, or by `main` and then opened by this change; `main` lists an index this change wrote identically too. The first writer's open of an index `main` wrote takes about 0.7 s, and later opens 2 ms, as on `main`. Writing the 500,000 symbol rows alone takes about 35% more CPU (19.7 to 21.0 s of user and system time, against 14.5 to 15.9 s, three runs each), for the extra index, and that synthetic database, which holds nothing but symbols, is 8.7% larger (323 MB against 297 MB). The index format is migrated in place and no stored fact changes, so no semantics version moves and an index stays current.
- A Rust path now reads a name in the namespace it uses it in (#643). A module can hold a type and a value of one name: beside a braced `struct Entry { .. }` that `store` defines, its `pub use crate::ledger::*;` brings in `ledger`'s `fn Entry`, and rustc reads the call `store::Entry()` as that function. The index proved the struct instead, an `Exact` `CALLS` edge to an item the call cannot name, and a call path to an enum, a trait or a type alias did the same. Types (modules, structs, enums, unions, traits, type aliases) and values (functions, constants, and the constructors of tuple and unit structs) are now told apart: the parser records which structs, enums, unions and type aliases are types alone, a call path reaches only a value, and a type path, or a path through one to its associated function, only a type. An item a module defines shadows what a glob beside it brings in only in the namespaces it occupies: `store::Entry()` above reaches `ledger::Entry`, a tuple `struct Entry(u8)` in `store` stays the value its constructor is, and beside a `fn Entry` in `store` and a glob that brings in `ledger`'s `struct Entry`, `store::Entry::new()` now reaches `new` of that struct, where it had no edge. Macros are a namespace of their own that no edge resolves in. A `use` still binds the one item it reads, as before, and a bare call through an import still needs that item to be a function.
- A Rust path is now followed through an enum's variants, a module a `use` renames, and the `use` declarations of an inline `mod` block (#641). `pub use Shape::*;` was a glob the index could not follow, so every other name a glob brings into the module it sits in stayed unresolved; it now brings in the enum's variants and nothing else. A path to a variant (`use crate::shapes::Shape::Circle;`, or `use crate::shapes::Circle;` through the glob) gets a file-level `IMPORTS` edge to the file declaring the enum, but binds no item and gives a call no edge, since a variant is not an item the index records. After `pub use inner as facade;`, `crate::facade::run()` and `use crate::facade::run;` had no edge; the renamed module is now followed within its crate, one step longer, and the one-segment `use` itself reaches `inner`'s file. A `pub use super::store::open;` written inside an inline `pub mod api { .. }` is followed for `crate::api::open()`, and for `engine::api::open()` from another crate, with `self` and `super` read from the block; an import path through an inline block also reaches the items the block defines. Each follows the visibility and ambiguity rules of other re-exports (#476). Not followed: a glob written inside an inline block, whose macros the parser does not record; a renamed module of another crate; and a path through a renamed module or an inline block below a module whose file configuration selects. A call path reaches a renamed module one segment deep (`crate::facade::run()`); an import path is read to any depth.
- Measured for #641 and #643 with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, keyed by edge type and endpoints, on this repository and the thirteen published Rust crates of #476's measurement. 42 authoritative edges were added, each where `main` had no edge or only an unproven one: 11 `CALLS`, 21 `IMPORTS` and 10 `USES_TYPE`, on six crates (15 on the Unix API binding crate, 14 on the syscall crate, 6 on the date and time crate, 4 on the atomics crate, 2 on the async combinator crate and 1 on the async runtime); this repository's and the other seven crates' authoritative graphs are unchanged. They come from imports through inline `mod` blocks (a type and its methods reached through `use crate::sys::clock::timer::Spec;`), a module a `use` renames (`pub(crate) use runtime_v2 as runtime;`), variants imported through a re-exported enum, and a call to a function a glob brings in beside a private module file of the same name, which is a type and not the value the call names. Six of the 21 `IMPORTS` edges are a `use` of an item of the file's own inline block: the path now resolves inside the importing file, which the file-level edge does not record, so the syntax edge keeps the module proof it has for any other such path. 3 authoritative syntax `IMPORTS` edges lost their module proof because the resolver now answers the same line with a file: `use runtime_v2 as runtime;` and two `cfg`-gated `use platform_a as platform;` lines reach the module's file. No other authoritative edge was lost or retargeted. Heuristic edges moved by 29 added and 10 removed, none authoritative. A hand check of all 42 new edges against the source found none wrong. Indexing CPU time, the median of three alternating runs per tree on a shared machine, ranged from 11% less to 4% more on this repository and five of the crates. The relationship conformance corpus passes (233 compact cases, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic); with `main`'s resolver, 10 of the 14 new cases fail.
- A Rust path through a `use` re-export now resolves (#476). After `pub use auth::issue_token;` in the crate root, `use crate::issue_token; issue_token(user)` and `crate::issue_token(user)` had no `CALLS` edge, the import bound no symbol, and the file-level `IMPORTS` edge stayed unresolved, so `ok impact --file src/auth.rs` listed neither caller; only `use crate::auth::issue_token;` was proven. A path is now followed through the `use` declarations at the top level of the module it names, read with the module tree and edition rules of any `use` path: named, grouped and `as` re-exports, chains of them up to eight steps, and globs. From the module's own crate any such `use` counts, a private one included, since only code below the module can name what it brings in; from another crate only `pub use` does, now for a call path through a crate name as well as for an import. A glob brings in a name only where it reaches an item it can see (a `pub` one from another crate, one not private to its module from its own crate), a named `use` shadows a glob, and every `use` that may supply the name must agree. Unresolved: two re-exports of one name that disagree, a glob the index cannot follow, a cycle, and a name only a glob brings into a module whose file invokes a macro at its top level that may expand to an item or a named `use` that shadows the glob (`cfg_if!`, `include!` or an unknown macro; a `thread_local!` only for the statics it declares; never `compile_error!`, an `assert!` or `const_assert!`, `include_str!`, `include_bytes!` or `doctest!`). An item the module defines shadows what a glob beside it brings in, as rustc reads it. A name the module defines while a named `use` beside it brings in another item of the name (a `cfg`-gated `use`) is reported ambiguous with both as candidates, and no longer proves the defined item. A re-export through a module whose file configuration selects keeps one unproven edge per file, as a path into the module does. The bare call carries the import rule `RustReexport` and strategy `rust_reexported_item_import`, the call path the strategies `rust_reexport_module` and `rust_reexport_member` (`rust_reexport_type` through a re-exported type), and the `IMPORTS` edge the strategy `rust-reexport`, reaching the file that declares the item. Three conformance cases that recorded the old gap now expect the edge or keep their intent another way, and fifteen new cases cover the rules above, including six that must not emit or stay ambiguous.
- Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, keyed by edge type and endpoints, on this repository and thirteen published Rust crates (a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, a date and time crate, an atomics crate, a random-number crate, an I/O event-loop crate, a lock-primitives crate, a C-bindings crate, a serialization crate, an async runtime, a regular-expression automata crate, an async combinator crate and a Unix API binding crate). 1,765 authoritative edges were added, each where `main` had no edge: 722 `CALLS`, 594 `IMPORTS`, 418 `USES_TYPE` and 31 `IMPLEMENTS`, most through types and functions a crate root or facade module re-exports. By corpus: 662 on the date and time crate, 226 on the event-loop crate, 224 on the async runtime, 196 on the automata crate, 135 on the syscall crate, 124 on the async combinator crate, 99 on this repository, 59 on the socket crate and 40 on the random-number crate; the other five crates' authoritative graphs are unchanged. 7 authoritative edges that `main` proved on this repository through a glob `pub use` were removed: the glob is in a file whose top level invokes a macro that defines items, which rustc lets shadow the glob, so the edge is not proven. No edge changed authority otherwise. Heuristic symbol-registry edges moved with the import bindings (1,712 added, 1,111 removed), none authoritative. No new edge goes from one alternative of a configuration choice into another, or from outside a choice into one alternative. A hand check of 30 of the new edges, sampled at random, against the source found none wrong. Indexing time was within 2% on this repository, the date and time crate and the C-bindings crate, and about 8% longer on the async runtime (9.1 s to 9.9 s). The relationship conformance corpus passes (219 compact cases, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic).
- Validation targets that tie on score and name are now chosen by repository position, not by the order the store lists them in (#468). The test selector behind context packs, plans, `ok tests` and `find_tests_for_change` ordered targets by score and then name, so two equally scored tests of one name in different files (`it_works`, `TestNew`) kept store order, and that order decided which of them a limit kept: a context pack takes up to five per primary file. Ties now fall through to the test file's path, the target's line range, then the file and target ids. Store order was file-id order, so a pack with more tied targets than its limit can now list different ones: on a fixture with seven equally matching `settles_ledger` tests in `tests/ledger_a.rs` to `tests/ledger_g.rs`, the pack keeps `a` to `e` rather than five picked by file id. Two store reads no longer lean on write order or the query plan: test targets for a set of files come back by file id and id, and a file's symbols of one name in source order (line range, then id), which on this repository is the order both returned before. Two fresh indexes of this repository on the base commit, with and without git history, already gave identical context, search and impact output for the 50 benchmark tasks and identical index contents apart from timestamps and the root-derived repository id; the reordering #468 reported came from the search index and was removed by #490 and #527. This change closes the order dependence an audit of the context, ranking, test-selection and impact paths still found.
- Plan validation targets that tie on selection tier, confidence and name are now cut by repository position, not file id (#644). `ok plan` and MCP `plan_change` group candidate tests by file id and cap the list at eight, so targets tied across files kept file-id order, a hash of the path, and that order decided which of them the cap kept and which went to `validation_omitted_ids`. Ties now fall through to the test file's path, the target's line range, then the file and target ids, the same order the test selector uses since #468. A plan with more tied targets than the cap can now keep different ones: with nine equally matching `settles ledger` tests in `tests/ledger_1.rs` to `tests/ledger_9.rs`, the plan keeps `1` to `8` and discloses `9` as omitted. `ok verify` is unaffected: it caps nothing, and it matches the saved plan's validation and omitted ids by id.
- `ok verify` and MCP `verify_change` now report a `missing_test` finding at the test file's repository path (#646). The finding's `path` held the recommended target's file id, a hash of the path that names no file; every other finding kind carried a real path. It is now looked up from the index the way plan validation targets are since #644, and is absent when the index does not know the file. A `validation_command_pending` warning had the same fault and the same fix: it now names the planned test's file by path, and carries no path for a target with no indexed file, such as a test a change contract names, where it used to carry the test's name as a path. `ok architecture overview` (and `clusters`, `hotspots`, `drift`, `boundaries`) listed each route/service boundary as `<endpoint> in <file id>`; it now gives the file's path, or the endpoint alone when the file is not indexed.
- A Rust type written as a `crate::`, `self::` or `super::` path is now read, for `USES_TYPE` and for a receiver's type (#637). `fn go(e: crate::model::Entry) { e.save(); }` gave `go` no `USES_TYPE` edge and `e.save()` no `CALLS` edge, while the relative spelling `model::Entry` has been read since #632. Such a path is now read as a call path through a type is, with its configuration reading: through a module whose file configuration selects, each file's type is a candidate, unproven, from outside the choice, and a path written inside one alternative reads its own. A type written as a crate-name path is still looked up by name only.
- A method call through a field of a Rust struct is now read through the type the field declares (#630). `self.s.m()` on a field `s: S`, with `S` imported from another module, had no edge, though a method call on a local or parameter of type `S` did. Each named field of a `struct` is now a binding in the struct's scope with its written type, and `self.field.m()`, `value.field.m()` and longer chains such as `self.a.b.m()` are read through it: the receiver's first segment is `self`, read as the type its `impl` names, or a binding whose type the index proves; each struct on the way must be one type, not reached through a module whose file configuration selects; and each field's type is read from the struct's own scope and file, with that file's imports. References and generic arguments of the field's type are read through as for a binding; `Box<S>` and `Option<S>` are not an `S`. The edge carries the `receiver_type` proof with strategy `rust_field_declared_type`. A field declared more than once (`#[cfg]`-gated fields), one typed by a type parameter of the struct, and a field of a type the index cannot resolve reach no candidate. Fields of an enum variant, a union or a tuple struct are not read, and a field's type is not read as a `USES_TYPE` of its struct.
- Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, keyed by edge type and endpoints, on this repository and eleven published Rust crates (a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, a date and time crate, an atomics crate, a random-number crate, an I/O event-loop crate, a lock-primitives crate, a C-bindings crate, a serialization crate, an async runtime and a regular-expression automata crate). 610 authoritative edges were added, each where `main` had no edge: 515 `CALLS` through struct fields, and 47 `CALLS` and 48 `USES_TYPE` through `crate::`, `self::` and `super::` type paths. By corpus: 383 on the automata crate, 108 on this repository, 67 on the async runtime, 31 on the syscall crate, 14 on the event-loop crate, 3 on the lock-primitives crate, 2 on the date and time crate and 2 on the socket crate; the other four crates' graphs are unchanged. No edge was removed or retargeted, no edge changed authority, and no edge that is not authoritative was added. No new edge goes from one alternative of a configuration choice into another: every new edge written inside the syscall crate's mounted backends stays in its own backend or reaches a file outside every alternative. A hand check of 30 of the 610 edges, sampled at random, against the source found none wrong. The relationship conformance corpus passes (204 compact cases, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic). `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v39` and `PARSER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is `tier1-parser-semantics-v16`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- A Rust `use` path whose first segment is a module the file declares, rather than `crate`, `self` or `super`, is now bound, and a type written as a path from such a module is now read (#632). Since the 2018 edition `use sys::imp::f;` in a file that declares `mod sys;` is `use self::sys::imp::f;`, in the 2015 edition it is `use crate::sys::imp::f;` wherever it is written, and `pub use error::Error;` in a crate root that declares `mod error;` re-exports that module's item in either, but the index bound none of them: a call, a method call, a declared type or an implemented trait through such an import had no edge, and the file had no `IMPORTS` edge for it. Ingest now reads the package's edition from its manifest (`[package] edition`, `edition.workspace = true` through `[workspace.package]`, and 2015 when the package sets none). In a 2015 package such a path is read as its `crate::` path when the crate root declares `mod` items of its first segment. Since 2018 it is read as its `self::` path when the file declares `mod` items of that name at its top level and no other item of the name in the type namespace, the `use` is written at that level or in a function body below it, and no scope between declares or imports the name or holds a glob that may supply it. The import is then bound, and followed for the file-level `IMPORTS` edge, as the `self::` spelling is, configuration choices included. A first segment that also names a crate the package can name (a dependency its manifest declares, `std`, `core` or `alloc`) may start from either, so the import stays unbound and its `IMPORTS` edge unresolved, and a call through it is reported ambiguous, with the module's item as its candidate and no edge, as a call path is since #626. A `use` written inside an inline `mod` block is not read this way by ingest; in a 2018 package a call through one is read by the resolver as the call path it spells. Where the index cannot read the edition, only a `use` in a crate root, where both editions name the same module, is read. A type written as a path whose first segment is a module in scope (`s: &sys::imp::S`, or the `sys::imp::S` of `sys::imp::S::new()`) is read through that path, for `USES_TYPE` and for the receiver of a method call, and keeps a candidate per file through a module whose file configuration selects. Not changed: a type written as a crate-name path is still looked up by name only, and has no edge; `crate::`, `self::` and `super::` type paths are read since #637.
- A relative Rust path written in a file a `path` attribute mounts inside one alternative of a module whose file configuration selects now resolves (#633). The module tree does not place such a file, so `util::g()`, `self::util::g()` or `super::f()` written in `unix/mod.rs`, mounted by `#[cfg_attr(unix, path = "unix/mod.rs")] mod imp;`, got no edge, while `crate::sys::imp::util::g()` from the same file has been proven since #624. The resolver now reads such a path off the module the file's route through the choices holds, here `sys::imp`, with the proofs and configuration reading of the `crate::` spelling. A file with no one route, such as one both alternatives mount, still gets none. A `mod sys;` declared inside an inline `mod b { .. }` is now placed at `src/b/sys.rs`, below the directory the blocks spell, as rustc reads it, so `sys::f()` written in the block and `crate::b::sys::f()` resolve, and `use` paths into it bind. A block with a `path` attribute, or a block inside a function body, still leaves the module files below it unplaced.
- Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, keyed by edge type and endpoints, on this repository and nine published Rust crates (a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, a date and time crate, an atomics crate, a random-number crate, an I/O event-loop crate, a lock-primitives crate, a C-bindings crate and a serialization crate). 190 authoritative edges were added, each where `main` had none: 137 `IMPORTS` (132 for `use` paths starting at a module in scope, 5 for `use` paths into a module file an inline block declares), 39 `CALLS` (11 through such imports, 23 through a type such an import or path names, 1 through an import inside a test function's body that the resolver reads, and 4 relative paths inside a backend directory a `path` attribute mounts) and 14 `USES_TYPE`. By corpus: 70 on this repository, 90 on the syscall crate, 21 on the date and time crate, 4 on the event-loop crate, 3 on the socket crate, 1 on the random-number crate and 1 on the C-bindings crate; the graphs of the other three crates are unchanged. No authoritative edge was retargeted, and one wrong one was removed: on the C-bindings crate a glob `use` whose first segment is an inline `mod` block of the importing file, named like the crate's own package, had an `IMPORTS` edge into the crate root, and it now stays unresolved. No edge was added from inside one alternative of a configuration choice into another. A hand check of 30 of the 190 edges, sampled at random, against the source found none wrong. Edges that are not authoritative: 3 corroborating `CALLS` on the syscall crate and 2 corroborating `USES_TYPE` on the socket crate, from paths into a configuration choice; and the symbol registry's heuristic edges change with the bindings it reads (41 `CALLS` and 74 `REFERENCES` added, 15 and 40 removed), none authoritative. On the 52 2015-edition packages of a local Cargo registry, no authoritative edge was lost or retargeted, 36 packages' graphs are unchanged, and 1,623 authoritative edges were added on 16 of them (1,260 `IMPORTS`, 298 `USES_TYPE`, 63 `CALLS` and 2 `IMPLEMENTS`), nearly all on a Windows bindings crate whose module files import from one another by `use shared::..;` and `use um::..;`; each of the 1,257 `IMPORTS` edges for a `use` path starting at a module names a module file of the crate root's `mod` item of that name, and a hand check of 30 of the 1,623, sampled at random, found none wrong. The relationship conformance corpus passes (188 compact cases, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic). `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v38`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`; parser output is unchanged.
- A Rust call path whose first segment is a module in scope, rather than `crate`, `self` or `super`, now resolves (#626). Since the 2018 edition `sys::imp::f()` in a file that declares `mod sys;` is `self::sys::imp::f()`, but the resolver read only paths starting with `crate`, `self`, `super` or a declared crate name, so the call got no edge while `crate::sys::imp::f()` from the same place got an `Exact` one. The first segment is now looked up in scope at the call: a `mod` item of the module around it, or of a module it reaches through `use super::name;` or `use super::*` (`mod tests` beside the file's `mod sys;`), starts the path there, and the path is read as that `self::` path, with its strategies (`rust_crate_qualified_*` into a module file, `rust_module_scope_*` into an inline `mod` block) and its proof: from module `a::b`, `sys::f()` names `a::b::sys`, never a `sys` the crate root declares. A path through a module whose file configuration selects keeps a candidate per file and no authoritative edge, as since #613, and one written inside an alternative reads that alternative, as since #624. A path of two or more segments whose first segment an explicit import in scope binds to a `crate::`, `self::` or `super::` path is read through it, from the scope the import is written in: `sys::imp::f()` after `use crate::sys;` is `crate::sys::imp::f()`. A first segment that names such a module and also a crate the package can name (a dependency its manifest declares, `std`, `core` or `alloc`) may start from either, so the call is counted as ambiguous, with the module's item as a candidate (strategies `rust_module_or_crate_path`, `rust_module_or_crate_member`), and gets no edge. A type or other item of the name, a block import of it, or a glob that may supply it is not read as the module, and a `mod` item inside a function body is not followed. Not changed: a one-segment path through an import (`sys::f()` after `use crate::sys;`) keeps its import proofs. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, keyed by edge type and endpoints: on this repository and on nine published Rust crates (among them a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, a date and time crate, an atomics crate, a C-bindings crate and a serialization crate), no edge was lost and no authoritative edge was retargeted, and two of the crates' graphs are identical. Authoritative `CALLS` edges were added only where such a path had none or only an unproven heuristic edge: 67 on this repository (41 new edges and 26 heuristic edges now proven), 2 on the syscall crate and 2 on a random-number crate. A hand check of 30 call sites sampled at random from the 80 behind the 71 new authoritative edges found none wrong. Paths into a module chosen by configuration added only unproven edges, `CALLS`, `High` and corroborating: 816 on the syscall crate (809 new edges and 7 heuristic edges now corroborated), 14 on the socket crate and 8 on the date and time crate. Nine authoritative edges into inline `mod` blocks, on four of the crates, keep their target and now carry the module-path proofs instead of the type-member ones the fallback gave them. The `ok demo` repository is this shape too: `auth::issue_token(..)` in its `src/lib.rs` beside `pub mod auth;` now has an `Exact` edge, so the plan for `token` in `docs/proof.md` has exact reference evidence and its confidence goes from `Medium` (0.74) to `High` (0.94). The relationship conformance corpus passes (167 compact cases, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic). `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v36` (v35 is left to an open change), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`; parser output is unchanged.
- A Rust path or `use` import written inside one alternative of a module whose file configuration selects now reaches that alternative alone (#624). Since #613 and #615 a call into such a module keeps one unproven `CALLS` edge per file, but a caller whose own file is one of the alternatives, or below one, reached the other alternatives too, though no build compiles both: with `#[cfg_attr(unix, path = "unix/mod.rs")]` beside `#[cfg_attr(windows, path = "windows/mod.rs")]`, `crate::sys::imp::util::g()` or `use crate::sys::imp::util::g;` written in `unix/mod.rs` reached `g` in `windows/util.rs` as well, because a file a `path` attribute mounts is not placed and the index could not tell which alternative it belongs to. Each file below a choice now records its route, the file every module from the choice down to it is compiled from; a caller with a route keeps only the candidates whose route agrees with its own, and its call is proven, `Exact` and authoritative, when that leaves one readable file for every choice on the path, as a call written in the placed default alternative already was. The file-level `IMPORTS` edge of such a `use` names that file too, where it named the placed default alternative's or nothing. A choice nested inside one alternative still leaves that alternative's files unproven; so do several `#[cfg]`-gated definitions of the name in the one file. A file both alternatives mount, one the tree also places as another module, or one a `mod` item outside every choice also reaches (`#[path = "sys/unix/util.rs"] mod uu;` in the crate root), has no route, nor has any file below it, and keeps every candidate, as a caller outside every alternative does; that last case holds for a file below the placed default alternative too, whose paths `main` proved through the placed tree though the extra mount compiles it on every build.
- A method call on a value of a Rust type imported through a module whose file configuration selects no longer proves the default file's method (#625). `use crate::sys::imp::S;` then `s.m()` (or `S::new()`) beside `#[cfg_attr(windows, path = "win.rs")] pub mod imp;` got one `Exact` edge into `imp.rs`, and `win.rs`'s `m` showed no caller. The call now keeps a `CALLS` edge to the method of each file's type at `High` confidence, whose `receiver_type` and `containing_type` proofs (strategies `rust_configured_receiver_type`, `rust_configured_type_member`) list the files, so it is corroborating, as the path and import spellings are. A `USES_TYPE` edge for a declared type and an `IMPLEMENTS` edge for the trait an `impl` names through such an import were likewise one `Exact`, authoritative edge into the placed file; each now keeps one edge per file that holds the type, at `High` confidence with import strategy `rust_configured_type_import`. Every proof those carry names the target and lists the files, so none is unique and they rank heuristic: visible to callers and impact, never authoritative. A type imported inside one alternative binds that alternative's type and stays proven.
- Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, keyed by edge type and endpoints: on this repository and on nine published Rust crates (among them a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, an atomics crate, a date and time crate, a C-bindings crate and a serialization crate), no authoritative edge was lost or retargeted, and the graphs of this repository and seven of the crates are identical. On the POSIX syscall crate, whose backend module is one of two `path`-mounted directories, the 542 corroborating `CALLS` edges from one backend into the other are gone (532 through imports, 10 through paths); 833 corroborating calls within one backend became authoritative (818 through imports, 15 through paths); and 30 method calls, 12 `USES_TYPE` edges and 91 `IMPORTS` edges written inside a backend, which had no edge because neither backend is placed, are now authoritative into their own backend. The symbol registry's heuristic name-match edges there change with the bindings it reads (304 `CALLS` and 275 `REFERENCES` added, 1897 raised from `Medium` to `High` confidence, 5 dropped); none is authoritative. On the socket crate, 8 heuristic `USES_TYPE` edges were added from functions whose parameter type `c_int` is imported through its platform module: only the Windows file declares that type, the Unix one re-exports the standard library's. No corpus had a method call on such an imported type from outside the choice; the tests and conformance cases do. The relationship conformance corpus passes (336 cases, 157 compact, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic) and matches the approved baseline.
- A call through a Rust `use` import into a module whose file configuration selects no longer proves the default file's item (#615). Since #613 a path into such a module, `crate::sys::imp::f()` beside `#[cfg_attr(windows, path = "win.rs")] mod imp;`, keeps one unproven `CALLS` edge per file that may hold it, but the same call written through an import still got one `Exact`, authoritative edge into the default file `imp.rs` and none into `win.rs`: `use crate::sys::imp::f; f();`, `use crate::sys::imp; imp::f();`, the `#[cfg]`-gated spelling of two `mod imp;` items, `use self::imp::f;` in the module declaring `imp`, and an import from another crate through the crate name, `use engine::sys::imp::f;` or `use engine::sys::f;` reaching the item through `pub use imp::f;` in `sys`. Now such an import names the item in each file, and a call through it keeps one `CALLS` edge per file at `High` confidence and never authoritative, as the path does: its import and qualified-name proofs (strategies `rust_configured_item_import` and `rust_configured_member`, or `rust_configured_receiver_import` and `rust_configured_receiver_member` for a call through an imported module) list the files as ambiguity, and the evidence message names them. An import written in one of those files that stays below the module (`use self::inner::g;` in `imp.rs`) is proven as before. A file that configuration selects and that is also declared as another module (`#[cfg_attr(unix, path = "../other.rs")] pub mod imp;` beside `mod other;`) stays a candidate through an import too, and `crate::other::f` still proves `other.rs` alone (#616). The path spelling in #616 already kept both candidates on `main`: the module-tree and resolver code it runs through is unchanged since #614 was merged, and the relative spelling `sys::imp::f()` from the crate root in the issue's example resolves no edge on `main` with or without a configuration choice; the import spelling was the one that dropped `other.rs`, and a test and conformance case now pin both. Not changed: the file-level `IMPORTS` edge of such a `use` still names the file the module tree places; a glob import into such a module binds no call, as a glob never does; and an in-crate path through a `pub use` is still not followed. The relative path `sys::imp::f()` written in the crate root gets no edge, with or without a configuration choice (#626). Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees: on this repository and on nine published Rust crates (among them a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, an atomics crate, a date and time crate, a C-bindings crate and a serialization crate), no authoritative edge was lost, added or retargeted, and the graphs of this repository and six of the crates are identical. Edges were added only where a call through such an import had none before, because no chosen file is placed: 1534 on the POSIX syscall crate (1455 through item imports, 79 through an imported module), 163 on the socket crate and 18 on the atomics crate, all `CALLS`, `High` and corroborating. None of the corpora had an `Exact` edge through such an import, which this change withholds; the tests and conformance cases do. The relationship conformance corpus passes (336 cases, 147 compact, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic) and matches the approved baseline. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v32`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- A Go type alias no longer ranks above the type it stands for, and an alias of an interface is an interface (#621). `type Entry = store.Entry` is one short line that repeats the type's name, so BM25 scored it above `type Entry struct { .. }` (10.78 against 10.16 on the Go alias fixture), and `ok symbol find` and MCP `search_symbols`, ordered by qualified name, listed `ledger::aliases::Entry` before `store::store::Entry`. The kind came from the alias's syntax node, which reads as any type declaration, so `type Source = store.Source` was a class although `store.Source` is an interface. Once every resolution pass has run, an alias the symbol registry placed at one repository type now takes that type's kind and names it in the new `Symbol::alias_of`, stored with the symbol in SQLite and in the Tantivy symbol record. `ok search` and `search_code` rank such an alias, when its target is in the same candidate pool and ranked below it, at the largest score below the target's, recorded as a `type_alias_below_target` score component that carries both results' evidence ids and names the target (`docs/ranking.md`). It is an ordering rule with no `ok.toml` weight, and lexical baseline ranking does not apply it. The other results of the alias's file that ranked above the target go just below the alias, with the same component, since search shows one result per file and the file would otherwise keep its rank through a neighbour (`type Blob = []byte` beside the alias, or `func NewEntry`) and show that line instead; a neighbour that declares a type of its own, which may be another alias's target, is never moved, so no alias ends above a target in the pool. An alias keeps its rank when its target is not in the pool, or when the query names the alias but not the target (`EntryID` for `type EntryID = store.ID`, or Go's `ledger.Entry` for an alias under `ledger/`), and a neighbour the query names keeps its rank as well. No result outside an alias's file moves relative to another. Symbol listings order a placed alias under its target's qualified name, just after the target, through a new `symbols.alias_order_key` column and a partial index, and page through the one order. An alias the registry could not place (`type R = io.Reader`, `type Raw = []byte`) keeps the kind its syntax gives it, names no target, and ranks and lists as before. Not changed: context packs, which fuse candidate streams by rank; an alias's file still ranks above the target when the alias's target is not in the pool, or through a neighbour the query names or one that declares a type; and `ok watch` rewrites only changed files' rows, so an alias in an unchanged file keeps the kind and target recorded when its file was last written until the next full `ok index`, as the registry's facts of unchanged files already do. A read surface on an index written before the column lists in plain qualified-name order; a store open adds the column. On 500,000 synthetic symbol rows, a page at offset 1,000 costs the same as before (22 ms from the `sqlite3` shell), and one at offset 200,000 rises from 22 ms to 390 ms, since the merge reads every row before the page. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees. The retrieval baseline is bit-identical and the workflow benchmark's per-case results are identical; the compact relationship conformance corpus passes (147 cases, no diagnostic, false positive or false negative). On a Go service (~2,400 files) indexing records the same symbols, analysis facts (92,911) and graph edges (142,054); 30 of its 56 aliases are placed, one of them an interface alias whose symbol and graph node change from class to interface, the only graph change. Searching each placed alias's own name (top 20), four aliases had ranked above their target and now rank directly below it; in one of them the alias's file holds a second alias whose target is not in the pool, and the file goes below the target with the alias as its line. Two aliases the query names exactly keep their rank above their targets, and in all 30 searches no file other than the alias's moved relative to another. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v37` (v34 is #628's and v36 is #631's; v33 and v35 were reserved for this change and are left unused), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- The symbol registry reads the package a Java or Go file declares instead of inferring it from the file's directory (#617, #620). A Java `static` import (`import static org.example.Constants.KEY;`) matches a member of the class named by the package the candidate's file declares, followed by the classes that enclose the member. A class in `src/Constants.java` declaring `package org.example;` is now `org.example.Constants`, so a static import of it links where it had no edge and a caveat wrongly said no candidate belongs to the class. A class whose directory spells `org/example` while it declares `package org.other;` is no longer taken for `org.example.Constants`. A file with no `package` declaration is in the unnamed package, which no import names. A Java import whose path lies inside a declared package, compared by whole segments, counts as the repository's, so in a layout where no directory spells `org`, a static import of `org.example.Constants` is no longer read as leading outside the repository, while `org.apache.commons.Widget` still is; another language's import is never read against a Java package. A static import still does not pick between two classes of the same simple name in different declared packages: the other is ruled out, but, as for any ruled-out candidate, the match stays ambiguous and the use gets no edge. Maven and Gradle layouts, whose directories mirror their packages, resolve as before. A Go `_test.go` file whose clause names the external test package (`package store_test` in `store/`) is not of the package an import of `store/` names, so no receiver places a member there. Go compiles a `_test.go` file only into its own directory's package tests, so its declarations are ruled out for a use in another directory, and an external test package's are also ruled out for a use outside that package. A ruled-out declaration is never the target. Beside another candidate of the name, the match stays ambiguous, as for any ruled-out item, but a ruled-out type alias leaves no ambiguity because it declares no type of its own. So `store.Entry{}` in `cmd/` links to `store.Entry` again beside the test package's `type Entry = audit.Record`, where since #618 it was ambiguous.
