A `_test.go` file of the package itself (`package store`) is of the package. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees. On a Java module of about 570 files, whose every file mirrors its package, registry facts are identical (23,038). On a Go service of about 2,400 files, registry facts went from 69,686 to 69,138: 548 removed and none added. Every removed edge ends at a declaration in a `_test.go` file of another directory, a reference Go cannot compile, and 30 of 30 sampled were wrong: parameters, locals and struct fields that share the name of a test helper in another package, and a method call on a mock matched to a function in another package's test file. Resolver facts are unchanged on both corpora. Not changed: a declaration a use cannot reach still makes a same-named candidate it can reach ambiguous, so a test helper shared by one package's test files stays unresolved when another package's tests declare the same name. The import resolver's own Java `IMPORTS` placement still reads directories. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v31` (v30 is taken by an open change), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- A Go member reached through a type alias declared in another package links again (#618). Since #605 the symbol registry reads a member such as `ledger.Entry` only in the package the receiver's import names, and when that package declares `type Entry = store.Entry` the index held no symbol there, so the member stayed unresolved with a caveat saying no candidate is in the named package. A Go type alias (`type X = pkg.Y`, alone or in a `type ( .. )` group) is now a type symbol whose signature spells the alias (`type Entry = store.Entry`), and the symbol record carries that signature, so `ok symbol find` lists it, and `ok search` shows its line, as an alias rather than a second declaration of the type (in search the one-line alias can rank just above the type it names). The parser records the alias's target, and the registry places it through the alias file's own imports (an unqualified name in the alias's own package, an alias of an alias followed on) and reads every match of the alias as its target: `ledger.Entry` links to `store.Entry`, and an alias and its target matched by one name are one candidate. An alias that a receiver's package rules out is not kept as a candidate, so another package's same-named alias does not make a receiver-placed member ambiguous. An alias whose target is not one repository type (a predeclared, composite, pointer or other module's type, a qualifier no import of its file binds, or a type declared once per build constraint) resolves to nothing, and its caveat names it: `` `ledger::aliases::Raw` is a Go type alias of `[]byte`, which the registry could not place at one repository type ``. No registry edge ends at an alias. Not changed: the resolver's own `USES_TYPE` edges are not read through aliases, so one proven from an annotation naming an alias now ends at the alias (the resolver had no edge there before); an alias's own declaration does not match the name it declares, so `type Entry = store.Entry` has no edge to `store.Entry`; and a bare name used in its own Go package is ambiguous beside a same-named alias elsewhere, as beside any same-named type. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same tree. On a Go service (~2,400 files), registry facts went from 69,542 to 69,686: 227 added and 83 removed. The 96 edges #605 withheld are back (members of one package reached through its aliases of another's types). Of the 227 added, 29 of 30 sampled were right; listing every group the sample missed found 14 wrong in all, 8 of them struct fields that share the name of an alias declared only for tests. All 83 removed were classified: 76 were wrong (47 struct field declarations matched to a same-named method, 12 generated alias declarations matched to mock types, 17 uses of a package's own alias matched to another package's type) and 7 were right (4 bare uses inside the declaring package, now ambiguous beside another package's same-named alias, and 3 alias declaration lines that no longer link to their target). Resolver-proven `USES_TYPE` rose from 10,358 to 10,408 and the graph gained 21 `USES_TYPE` edges into alias symbols, none lost. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v29` (v28 is taken by an open change), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- A Rust call path into a module whose file configuration selects no longer proves one file's item (#613). Such a module has more than one file, of which a build compiles one: `#[cfg_attr(windows, path = "win.rs")] mod imp;` beside `imp.rs`, or `#[cfg(not(windows))] mod imp;` beside `#[cfg(windows)] #[path = "win.rs"] mod imp;`. Before, `crate::sys::imp::f()` got one `Exact`, authoritative `CALLS` edge into the default file `imp.rs` and none into `win.rs`, and the reader was not told another file exists. Now the call keeps one `CALLS` edge per file that may hold the module (a path below it, such as `crate::sys::imp::inner::g()` or one through a type declared there, is read the same way), at `High` confidence and never authoritative: the module and qualified-name proofs (strategies `rust_configured_module`, `rust_configured_member`, `rust_configured_type`) list the files as ambiguity, and the evidence message names them, including a `path` attribute the index cannot read. The resolution metrics count such a call as ambiguous. Every candidate is kept, not dropped, so callers and impact see the caller of either file's `f`: of `imp.rs`'s as a corroborated rather than a proven relationship, and of `win.rs`'s, which had no edge before. A path written inside one of those files that stays below the module (`self::inner::g()` in `imp.rs`) is proven as before. `cfg_attr(all(), path = ..)`, and a condition written beside its own `not(..)` on one `mod` item (`cfg_attr(unix, path = "u.rs")` with `cfg_attr(not(unix), path = "o.rs")`, compared as written, whitespace outside literals aside), now read as holding on every build, parsed or read off a file skipped for size, so the default location is no longer placed: before, `crate::sys::imp::f()` there got an `Exact` edge into a leftover `imp.rs` rustc never compiles. Any other set of conditions (`any(unix, not(unix))`, `unix` beside `not(any(unix))`), and a `path` set inside a nested `cfg_attr(unix, cfg_attr(feature = "x", path = ..))`, which holds on fewer builds than its outer condition, is still read as one that may fail. Not changed: a `use` import of an item in such a module still binds the placed default file, and a module whose alternative is an inline `mod` block is not read as a choice. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees: on this repository, and on nine published Rust crates that select module files with `cfg_attr` or `#[path]` (among them a POSIX syscall crate, a socket crate, an async runtime, a date and time crate, an atomics crate and a C-bindings crate), no authoritative edge was lost, added or retargeted, and the graphs of this repository and seven of those crates are identical. Edges were added only where a path into a module chosen by configuration had none before, because no chosen file is placed: 39 on the POSIX syscall crate (a path into its backend module reaches both of its backends) and 10 on the socket crate (a path into its platform module reaches both the Unix and the Windows file), all `CALLS`, `High` and corroborating. None of the corpora had an `Exact` edge into a default file beside a `cfg_attr` path, which this change now withholds; the layouts in the tests and conformance cases do. The relationship conformance corpus passes (336 cases, 140 compact, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic) and matches the approved baseline. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v26` (v25 is taken by an open change), so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index`.
- A Rust module whose every `path` attribute is a `cfg_attr` (`#[cfg_attr(windows, path = "../common.rs")] mod imp;`) is now also placed at its default location, `imp.rs` or `imp/mod.rs`, which rustc compiles it from whenever no condition holds (#608). Before, the index followed only the `cfg_attr` paths, so the default file was read as declared by no module. When another crate mounted the declaring file with `#[path]` (an integration test mounting `src/sys/mod.rs`, say), the default file was not marked as compiled into that crate too, and `crate::helper()` in `src/sys/imp.rs` kept an `Exact` `CALLS` edge to the library's `helper`; it is now left unresolved and counted in the `relationship_resolution` quality note. In the module's own crate, `self::` and `super::` paths from the default file, and paths into it such as `crate::sys::imp::f()`, now resolve as they already did for a `#[cfg(..)]`-gated `mod imp;` with no `path`. A `path` attribute that always applies (`#[path = ".."]`), alone or beside a `cfg_attr` one, still moves the module off its default location for good. The `mod`-item scan of a file skipped for exceeding `max_file_size` now reads the same form when the `cfg_attr` sits on a `mod name;` outside every block and its value is a plain string literal: it records the module name and each path, so a skipped `#[path]`-mounted file marks only the files it may compile instead of every file of its package. Any other `path` attribute still makes the scan give up, and a skipped crate root with a `cfg_attr` path is still read as able to declare any module below it. On this repository's own index, and on three published crates that use `cfg_attr` paths (a POSIX syscall crate of 317 Rust files, a date-time crate of 164 and an atomics crate of 54), no authoritative edge and no graph edge count changes; their default-location files already had their `crate::` paths read against the package, and none makes a `self::` or `super::` call into a changed placement. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v24`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` and must be rebuilt with `ok index`.
- A Rust file a `#[path]` attribute mounts, or one below such a file, that discovery skipped for exceeding `max_file_size` now has its `mod` items read, as a size-skipped crate root's already were, when marking the files the mounting crate compiles too (#610). With `tests/it.rs` mounting an oversized `src/m.rs` that declares `mod b;`, `crate::helper()` in `src/b.rs` got an `Exact` `CALLS` edge to the library's `helper` although rustc compiles `src/b.rs` into the test crate as well: the skipped file's declarations were never read, so nothing below it was marked. It is now left unresolved and counted in the `relationship_resolution` quality note. A mounted file that was not indexed and whose `mod` items cannot be read (excluded by a path policy, or holding a `path` attribute of its own) may now mount any file of its package into the mounting crate, and the note says how many such files marked one. The `mod`-item scan of a size-skipped file now uses the symbol registry's Rust code lexer instead of a second hand-written one; comments (line, block, nested, doc) and string, byte-string, raw-string and character literals are skipped as before, with lifetimes kept as code. On this repository's own index and on a large async-runtime crate (555 Rust files), each indexed at the default size limit and at `8kb`, no authoritative edge changes. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v22`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` and must be rebuilt with `ok index`.
- Rust trait `impl`s of traits defined outside the repository are counted as `external` in `resolution_quality.by_relationship.IMPLEMENTS`, not `unresolved` (#599). An impl is external when the trait's path, or the `use` that binds its first segment, names `std`, `core`, `alloc` or a dependency the package's manifest places outside the repository (a registry dependency the workspace root's `[patch]` or `[replace]` points at a `path` in the repository is not outside it), or when it names a standard-prelude trait (`Iterator`, `From<T>`) that nothing in scope imports or shadows; a path through `crate`, `self` or `super`, a crate the manifest does not declare, or an import the index cannot place stays `unresolved`. On a large async-runtime crate the unresolved `IMPLEMENTS` count went from 699 to 94, with 605 external; on this repository from 95 to 39, with 56 external. No edge changes: the external outcome only moves the count.
- A method call on a Rust binding annotated with a generic type or a reference now uses that type as the receiver (#599). `w: &Wrapper<u8>`, `x: Foo<u8>`, `m: &'a mut Foo` and `let v: Wrapper<u8> = ..` type their receiver as `Wrapper` or `Foo`; before, the annotation was looked up by its full text (and a `&mut Foo` parameter as `mut Foo`), which names no symbol, so the call stayed unresolved. `v: Vec<Foo>` is a `Vec`, never a `Foo`, and a tuple, slice, pointer, trait object or `impl Trait` still types nothing. Two `impl` blocks of different instantiations that each declare the method keep both candidates. A Rust closure is now its own scope, so a typed closure parameter (`|ctx: &mut Ring|`) no longer types a same-named variable the enclosing function uses after the closure; on this repository that removes 3 `CALLS` edges that were proven through such a leaked parameter.
- A Rust call path through a type, `crate::a::Engine::new()` or `engine::Engine::new()` through a crate the caller's package declares, now reaches that type's associated function (#599). The module-path resolver read `Engine` as a module and found nothing, since an `impl` member's qualified name is spelled from its file. The type must be one the path's module holds, placed in the crate the path names, and the member must be declared in an `impl` in the type's own file.
- Measured on the same trees with release builds of `main` and this change: proven `CALLS` rose from 1,986 to 2,018 on a large async-runtime crate (26 new authoritative edges, none lost or retargeted) and from 12,280 to 12,388 on this repository (91 new, 3 lost as described above, none retargeted) (#599).
- The symbol registry's unique-project-name strategy, which links a token to the only symbol of that name in the repository, now matches by name alone only a token whose place in the code lets it name that symbol. Most of its wrong edges came from local variables and parameters (`|test| test.id`), field names (`score: 0.1`, `Foo { field, .. }`, a Go `Key: value`), member accesses with no receiver evidence (`.join(..)`, `.contains(..)`), attributes (`#[test]`) and paths into another crate or an enum (`std::mem::take`, `GraphNodeType::File`). A lexical pass now gives each token a role, and the name-only strategies (unique project name, suffix import reachability, fuzzy) no longer match: an attribute or `@decorator` name (a Java `@Annotation` matches only a class or interface); a member access, unless its receiver is a plain name that names a directory, module or type the candidate is defined under (Go `store.NewEntry`, Java `Constants.ACCESS_KEY` with a capitalized receiver, Python `misc.format_size`; never on an `import`, `from`, `package` or `use` line); or a name the chunk binds as a local before the use (Rust `let`, `for`, closure and function parameters; JavaScript `const`, `let` and `var`; Python assignments, `for`, `as`, `lambda` and `def` parameters; Java typed declarations; Go `:=` and `var`; a `let`, `const`, `var`, `:=` or Python assignment binds only once its value is read, so `let config = config(dir);` still calls `config`; scoped by position in the chunk, not by block). A field or parameter name matches only a symbol of kind field. A Rust path tail matches only a symbol its qualifier leads to: a module or crate path (which may re-export) constrains nothing, a repository type constrains the match to that type's members, and a qualifier that names nothing in the repository (`std`, `serde_json`, an enum alias) matches nothing; `crate::`, `self::`, `super::` and `Self::` are unchanged. A name the file imports under that name from outside the repository (`use anyhow::Result;`, from the resolver's import model, so aliases are honored and a Java `static` import is read by its path) matches nothing either. These tokens still resolve through a direct import or the use site's own file, and a token left unresolved carries a caveat naming why (for example `member access without receiver evidence`). Python f-string replacement fields are now read as code, up to a `!r` conversion or `:` format spec (whose nested fields are code too); `{{`, `}}` and `\N{...}` escapes stay text. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees, sampling 30 removed and 30 remaining unique-project-name edges per corpus (Python: unique-project-name and fuzzy-fallback removals together). On this repository (Rust, where #592 already moved many cross-crate names to direct-import edges): unique-project-name facts fell from 14,523 to 1,925; 0 of 30 removed edges were right, and 29 of 30 remaining (the wrong one a match-arm binding, `Ok(current)`). On a Go service of about 2,400 files: 55,982 to 32,008; 7 of 30 removed were right (method calls through a receiver variable, `s.Save(..)`, and packages imported under another name) and 21 of 30 remaining. On a Java module of about 570 files: 9,233 to 5,059; 6 of 30 removed were right (instance method calls, `fs.openStream(..)`) and 23 of 30 remaining. On a Python ML library of about 400 files: 44,742 to 12,628; 1 of 30 removed was right and 26 of 30 remaining (the wrong ones are builtins such as `max` and `next` matched to a same-named repository function). On a Python package of about 140 files: unique-project-name and fuzzy-fallback facts fell from 4,791 to 1,031, with 1 of 30 removed right; its remaining fuzzy-fallback edges (a token contained in a longer name, `logger` to `install_warning_logger`) sampled 0 of 19 right and are not changed here. Weighting those samples by the counts, the strategy's estimated precision rises from about 50% to 70% on the Go service, 51% to 77% on the Java module, 27% to 87% on the Python library and 13% to 97% on this repository, at a cost of roughly 5,500, 800 and 1,000 correct edges on the first three. F-string fields add a few facts (7 on this repository, 64 and 203 on the Python corpora), mostly direct-import and same-file; their tokens count toward the pass's 80-token limit per chunk, which pushes some later tokens out (9 same-file calls here). On this repository's graph, edges fell from 58,209 to 50,797, all unique-project-name (`CALLS` 2,678 to 365, `REFERENCES` 6,213 to 1,114); no other edge class changed. Registry edges stay heuristic. The relationship conformance corpus is unchanged: 336 cases (117 compact) with no false positive, false negative or diagnostic and the same observation digests; the retrieval baseline and every workflow benchmark case are unchanged. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v16`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#582).
- The symbol registry no longer links a member to the repository's same-named package or class when the file imports the receiver, or the name, from somewhere else. Since #582 a member such as `pkg.Name` matched by name alone wherever its receiver named a directory, module or type the candidate is defined under, whatever the receiver was imported from. A receiver the file imports from outside the repository (by the test a plain name already had) now matches nothing, in every language: a Python `import numpy as np` no longer links `np.array_widget()` to a repository module named `np`. A Go receiver is read by its whole import path. Under a module a `go.mod` of the repository declares (the longest module path that prefixes the import, nested modules included; of two `go.mod` files declaring one module, the importer's own, else the shallowest), the rest of the path below the manifest's directory is the package directory exactly, so a module path without a dot (`module myapp`) is the repository's and a longer directory that also ends the path does not win. A `go.mod` under a `testdata` or `_`- or `.`-prefixed directory, which the go command ignores, declares no module, and a module whose directory holds no package at the path gives way to the next that does. A path under no declared module is another module's package or the standard library's and matches nothing (vendored code is not indexed, so that includes vendored packages). With no module declared, the longest repository directory the path ends with stands in. So a receiver imported from another module whose last path segment equals a repository package, or from a sibling package whose file shares the receiver's name, no longer links there, and a package imported under an alias now matches by the alias. `go.work` files and `replace` directives are not read (a workspace member or `replace` target is placed through its own `go.mod`), and a Go receiver no import binds under its name (a package whose name is not its path's last segment, imported without an alias) is matched as before. A Java `static` import that is not from outside matches only a member of the class its path names, package included (read from the file's directories, so a file whose directory does not mirror its `package` declaration is not matched), as a path through a type already did: `import static <package root>.<library class>.name`, or a same-named class in another package, no longer links `name` to a repository member. A token left unresolved says why (`the member's receiver is imported from outside the repository`, `no registry candidate is in the package the receiver's import names`, `no registry candidate belongs to the class its static import names`). Measured with release builds of `main` and this change over the same trees. On a Go service of about 2,400 Go files, registry facts went from 69,360 to 69,542, all in unique-project-name member edges: 354 removed and 536 added. Of the removed, classified exhaustively, 241 had a receiver imported from another module and 113 a receiver whose import names another directory of the repository; 258 were wrong and 96 reached the right type only because the imported package declares a type alias of that name (`type Entry = other.Entry`), which the index does not hold as a symbol, so those 96 are a loss and their caveat names the imported package, where the alias is. The 536 added are members of packages imported under an alias or from a directory whose name is not the package's; 30 of 30 sampled were right, as were 30 of 30 sampled remaining import-bound receiver edges. On a Java module of about 570 files, facts went from 23,145 to 23,038: all 107 removed were names statically imported from a class that is not in the repository (a parent project's test-utility class, a mocking library), 0 of 30 sampled right, and 30 of 30 sampled remaining static-import edges were right. On the other corpora measured (a Python project of about 1,800 Python files, a TypeScript project of about 600 files, and this repository) the registry facts are identical; other codebases in any language can lose edges through receivers imported from outside. The relationship conformance corpus passes unchanged (336 cases, 140 compact, no false positive, false negative or diagnostic, matching the approved baseline), the retrieval baseline matches exactly, and every workflow benchmark case scores as on `main`. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v27`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#605).
- A Rust trait `impl` member whose trait is written as a `self::`, `super::` or `crate::` path, or whose implementing type is a reference or smart pointer, no longer records `Public` when the file bounds it (#570). `impl Store for &Hidden`, `&mut Hidden`, `Box<Hidden>`, `Rc<Hidden>`, `Arc<Hidden>` and `Pin<..>` of them with a private `Hidden` now record `Private`, and `impl crate::Cache for Gen<u8>` with a `pub(crate) trait Cache` records `Crate`. An `impl` type parameter that shares a name with a private type the file declares (`impl<Hidden> Cache for Box<Hidden>`) is a blanket impl and no longer takes that type's visibility; before, it recorded `Private` for the bare form. A path is followed only into a module of the same file: `super::` past the file's top level, `crate::` outside a Cargo default crate root (`lib.rs`, `main.rs`, `bin/*.rs`), and longer module paths still record `Public`, as does a trait or type declared in another file (#600). The type bound states who can name the implementing type, not who can reach the method: a private type returned as `impl Trait` or `Box<dyn Trait>` is callable from outside. Indexing this repository changes no stored visibility. The parser semantics version is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v9`, so existing indexes report stale Rust parser facts until rebuilt.
- A graph edge that several writes describe now shows its earliest call site, and the graph builder folds those writes the same way whatever order they arrive in (#597). When writes of one `(from, to, type)` edge had equal rank, `GraphBuffer` kept the evidence with the smallest evidence id, a hash of the edge's target node, so the file and line an edge showed was an arbitrary one of its sites and moved whenever node identity changed (#595 moved it on 1,534 edges without changing any edge's meaning). The representative evidence is now the highest source rank and confidence, then the earliest site (path, then line; evidence with a site before evidence without one), then the smaller evidence source and id. Every site stays listed in `call_sites`/`reference_sites`. A field the representative write leaves unset (a property, `source_pass` or another provenance field) used to be filled from whichever write was folded first and now takes the smallest value any write gave, as node merges do since #591. On this repository's graph (62,739 edges, release builds of `main` and this change on the same tree), 10,591 edges list two or more sites: 5,522 (52.1%) showed the earliest before and 10,566 (99.8%) after; the other 25 are `USES_TYPE` edges whose evidence carries no site. 5,044 edges show a different site and 5,045 carry a different evidence id (one `DEPENDS_ON` edge between two manifests, declared both as a dependency and a dev-dependency, now names `open-kioku-cargo-manifest/dependency` as its source where it named the dev-dependency); no edge was added or removed, no edge's type, confidence, source type or window rank changed, no node changed, and the retrieval baseline, workflow benchmark cases and relationship conformance corpora are unchanged. `GRAPH_EMISSION_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-graph-emission-v3`, so an index built before this change reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` rebuilds it; an incremental writer that reconciled such an index instead would rewrite each changed edge once, since its `content_hash` differs.
- A graph node an analysis fact points at (an unresolved call target, an imported module, an implemented interface, a package, a topic or queue) no longer merges with another whose name differs only in case (#595). Its id hashed the lowercased label, so `use tempfile::tempdir` and `use tempfile::TempDir` imported one `Module` node, and a registry `CALLS` edge to the function `pruned_marker` landed on the node of the constant `PRUNED_MARKER`; the node showed whichever spelling was written first and every edge to either name. The label is now hashed as spelled for every node kind that takes this id, since code identifiers, module paths, package import names and topic and queue names are case-sensitive. `DatabaseTable` labels still fold case, because unquoted SQL identifiers compare without it and the same table is spelled `Orders` in a JPA mapping and `orders` in a traced statement. Every such node whose label holds an uppercase letter gets a new id, so the stable-identity semantics version moves to `stable-identity-v2` and an existing index reports `rebuild_required` until `ok index .` is run. On this repository's own graph (release builds of `main` and this change, same source) the index went from 14,749 to 14,751 nodes and 58,905 to 58,906 edges: two nodes split (`tempfile::tempdir` from `tempfile::TempDir`, and `pruned_marker` from `PRUNED_MARKER`), two edges moved to the node they name and one import edge that the merge had folded into another now exists; 1,534 other edges keep their type, endpoints and confidence but report a different evidence line, because when several uses of one target tie the stored evidence is chosen by an id that hashes the node id. The retrieval baseline, the workflow benchmark (all 20 cases identical), and the relationship conformance corpus (full and compact, no diagnostics, false positives or false negatives) are unchanged apart from the recorded semantics fingerprint.
- Rust methods and trait implementations in an `impl` for a generic type are linked to the type (#593). The parser recorded the owner of `impl<'a> PlanEngine<'a>` or `impl<T> Wrapper<T>` by its written text, `PlanEngine<'a>`, which names no symbol, so `PlanEngine::new(...)`, `wrapper.get()` and the `IMPLEMENTS` edge of `impl<T> Clone for Wrapper<T>` stayed unresolved within and across crates for every type with lifetime or type parameters. The owner is now the type's path without its generic arguments; an `impl` for a reference (`&T`) or a trait object (`dyn Trait`) still links to neither. Two `impl` blocks for different instantiations that declare the same method name (`impl Wrapper<u8>` and `impl Wrapper<u16>`) now both belong to `Wrapper`, and a call to that name keeps both candidates and no authoritative edge, since the index does not match type arguments. The parser semantics version is now `tier1-parser-semantics-v8`, so existing indexes report stale Rust parser facts until rebuilt.
- `ok watch` no longer keeps a stored graph node's old content when it changes under the same id, and the graph builder merges the writes that describe one node the same way whatever order they arrive in (#591). The incremental writer kept every stored node whose id the new graph held, so a node whose content depends on facts from other files kept it until the node was deleted: reproduced by a new test, committing two files whose content the index already held left their file nodes' `source_pass` naming only the earlier commit where a clean index names both, and #591 reports two file nodes of this repository's own index differing from a clean index after a few commits under `ok watch`. Each `graph_nodes` row now stores `content_hash`, a hash of the JSON document the row holds, and the reconcile step keeps a stored node only when its hash matches the new node's; otherwise the row is deleted and inserted again. Edges name their endpoints through the string dictionary, not through node rows, so a rewritten node keeps its edges. The column is added by the first writer open of an existing index, with no backfill; the incremental writer decodes a row without a hash the first time it reconciles it, then records the hash or rewrites the row. `GraphBuffer::upsert_node` used to take `label`, `source_pass` and the other provenance fields from the first write that set them and properties from the last, and to keep `ambiguity` and `quality_notes` in arrival order; it now takes the label from a write that carries the node's file or symbol id when there is one, the smallest value for every other field and for a property two writes disagree on, and sorted unions for `ambiguity` and `quality_notes`. A clean index can therefore store different node content than before: on this repository's graph (14,701 nodes, 58,596 edges) 720 nodes changed, 717 in `source_pass` alone (the smallest of the sources that point at the node, where it was the first one read), one `Module` node's label (`tempfile::TempDir` where it was `tempfile::tempdir`, two spellings sharing one id) and two `Endpoint` nodes' `raw_path` property. No node was added or removed, no edge changed, and the retrieval baseline, workflow benchmark gates and relationship conformance corpus are unchanged. Measured with release builds of `main` and this change on this repository's graph (a loaded machine), reconciling an unchanged graph took 236-278 ms before and 237-272 ms after, 14 runs each; the first update of an index written before this change also decodes every node once (389-747 ms here, then back to the steady cost).
- `ok watch` no longer keeps a stored graph edge's old proofs when they change under the same id (#581). An edge id hashes only the edge type and its endpoints, and the incremental writer kept every stored edge whose id the new graph held, so an edge whose content depends on more than its endpoint files and its evidence path kept its old content until it happened to be deleted. Reproduced with `ok watch`: after `src/a.rs` and `src/b.rs` were committed together, an update triggered by another file kept their co-change edge at `Medium` confidence naming only the first commit, where a clean index records `High` and both commits. A relationship proof whose candidate count another file's change alters is exposed the same way (covered by a storage test, not reproduced through `ok watch`), and relationship authority, bounded windows and every other reader of a stale row see its old evidence. Each `graph_edges` row now stores `content_hash`, a hash of everything the row holds except its id and `indexed_at`, and the reconcile step keeps a stored edge only when its hash matches the new edge's; otherwise the row is deleted and inserted again from the new graph, which recomputes its window rank. Unchanged edges still keep their row and `indexed_at`. The column is added by the first writer open of an existing index, with no backfill; the incremental writer decodes a row without a hash the first time it reconciles it, then records the hash or rewrites the row. Measured on this repository (58,039 edges, release build, a loaded machine), reconciling an unchanged graph took 92-170 ms before and 227-430 ms after, most of it hashing every edge the new graph holds; the first update of an index written before this change also decodes every edge once (about 4.8 s here). A full `ok index` hashes each edge as it inserts it, and its run-to-run spread on this machine (14-56 s) hid any difference.
- A failed prune of the semantic vector store or stored context handles no longer shares `quality.pending_deleted_content_clearing` with the database's compaction (#564 added it there). Sharing it made the next `ok index` after a prune-only failure also `VACUUM` the whole database, and made every `ok watch` file event re-run the prune (re-reading the vector store's whole `ids.json`) until the next `ok index` or watcher start, even after a prune had succeeded, because a successful prune did not clear the flag and an incremental event carries it forward. It is now `quality.pending_derived_store_pruning`: set when a prune fails, cleared by the next prune that succeeds, and carried to each manifest a writer publishes until then, so `ok status`, `repo_status` and `ok doctor` (a new `derived_stores` warning, with its own next step) keep reporting it; `ok index` prints it as a `derived stores:` line. The `deleted_content` wording again names only the database file and its write-ahead log. A pending prune is retried by the next `ok index` or watcher start, and by a watch event that removes a path, not by every event. A run that dropped a path (`ok index`, either `ok watch` writer) and every `ok snapshot import` publish their manifest with the record already set and clear it once their prune succeeds, so a run killed between publishing and pruning (for example while a reader holds `.ok/context.sqlite` and the prune waits) stays reported instead of leaving a clean status. `ok retrieve-context` and MCP `retrieve_context` now refuse a handle that quotes a file the published index no longer holds, with an error (exit 1, JSON-RPC `-32000`) that names neither the file nor its text, so such a handle is not served even before a prune deletes it; a handle stored before paths were recorded that carries no file range is refused the same way. A document the index holds counts as held for both: the prune #564 added judged handles against indexed files only, so every `ok index` deleted handles quoting a README's or other document's sections. New public API: `ContextHandleStore::retrieve_indexed`, `clearing::PathsBefore::dropped_indexed_path` and `SqliteStore::indexes_path`. `ok watch` now prunes before its semantic refresh rather than after, because settling the record rewrites the manifest the refresh fingerprints. Measured on a synthetic store (method and numbers in the pull request), a watch event that edits one file while a prune is recorded as pending no longer reads the vector store. A prune stopped between replacing `ids.json` and `embeddings.cache` left the removed targets' cache entries (target id, content hash and vector; no text or path) until the next `ok semantic index`: the generation's `pruned.json` marker is now marked complete only after every file is rewritten, and the next prune finishes an incomplete one by dropping cache entries whose target is no longer in `ids.json`. A marker written before this has no completion record, so the first prune after upgrading reads that generation's cache once. `ok semantic status` reports a pruned generation's `vector_count` and `indexed_count` as 0, since it has no vector index until rebuilt (they read as the number of kept cache entries before), names the kept embeddings in a note, and no longer adds "semantic configuration changed" to a pruned generation's rebuild reasons when the configuration did not change (#585).
- Rust imports and calls across the crates of a Cargo workspace are resolved. `use engine::PlanEngine` in a package whose manifest declares `engine` (by `path`, directly or through `[workspace.dependencies]`, in `[dependencies]`, `[dev-dependencies]` or, for the build script, `[build-dependencies]`, including target-specific tables and renames) binds to the item in that package's library module tree, following its `pub use` re-exports, crate-root and nested, named and glob, and `engine::plan::build()` resolves there too. `get_references`, callers, `explain_symbol`, `dependency_path` and the relationship graph now carry exact cross-crate `CALLS` and `IMPORTS` edges where they had none; a package that does not declare the dependency, or declares a package of the same name in another workspace, gets none. Indexing stores the Cargo package model as facts on each `Cargo.toml` (`open-kioku-cargo-manifest/*`, with `DEPENDS_ON` edges between manifests), and `ok impact` / MCP `impact_analysis` read those and the stored import resolutions instead of parsing manifests and `use` paths at query time, so two workspaces with a package of one name are no longer conflated; reasons name a package by crate name and manifest directory, and count `use` declarations into the changed crate that resolved to no file. A Rust call path (`crate::task::yield_now()`, `other_crate::task::yield_now()`) or a call through an imported module (`use crate::task; task::yield_now()`) no longer proves an edge to a `mod yield_now;` declaration that shares the item's name. The symbol registry looks an import's target symbols up by name instead of scanning the target file per token, which cross-crate resolution would otherwise have made several times slower to index. A lowercase `path::f()` call is read as a module path rather than a value, so `engine.run()` on a closure parameter, loop or pattern binding named like a crate is never taken for `engine::run()`; a package's library code and build script do not name the package's own crate. Relationship resolver semantics v15 and parser semantics v7: an existing index is rebuilt by the next `ok index` (#587).
- Bounded graph edge reads cost what their window holds, not the node's degree (#574). Since #562 a window is ordered by evidence tier, which lives in the typed relationship proofs SQL cannot read, so the SQLite store decoded and sorted every edge of a node whose edges carry an extension record but are not proven, such as a symbol-registry hub. Each edge now persists its window rank (`graph_edges.window_rank`, the tier and confidence folded into one integer by the new `open_kioku_core::graph_edge_window_rank`), computed by the one insert every writer uses, and four indexes on `(endpoint, window_rank, id)` and `(endpoint, edge_type, window_rank, id)` replace the four endpoint indexes, so `neighbors`/`neighbor_window`, `edges_by_type_for_node` and `graph_edges_between` are index range scans cut at their limit. Measured with release builds on the same indexes (median of 15 reads, 20-edge windows), a function with 30,000 inbound symbol-registry `CALLS` edges: `neighbor_window` 478 ms to 3.9 ms, the inbound `CALLS` page 421 ms to 0.12 ms; a function with 30,000 proven inbound `CALLS` edges: 152 ms to 4.0 ms and 97 ms to 0.27 ms; this repository's six largest hubs (543 to 1,296 edges): `neighbor_window` 6 to 23 ms to 0.3 to 0.9 ms. Every window returned the same edges in the same order as before. The cost is index size: the rank indexes carry the edge id, which is the order's tiebreak, and grow a fresh index of this repository from 270.5 MB to 289.7 MB (+7%; 64,341 edges) and of the 30,000-edge synthetic repositories by 9 to 10%. Building the four wider indexes adds to the graph write: indexing user CPU time rose by 0 to 2% in paired runs on this repository and the 170,840-edge synthetic repository, and an independent review measured about 5% more full-index time; wall-clock comparisons on the shared build host were too noisy to state more precisely. The storage schema version (`user_version` 4) is unchanged. The next writer open (`ok index`, `ok init`, `ok watch`, `ok snapshot import`) ranks an existing index in place, in one transaction, decoding every edge once and then building the rank indexes (1.8 s for this repository's 64,174 edges, 4.2 s for 170,840; about 25 µs per edge); `ok status`, `ok doctor` and the read surfaces rank nothing, and until a writer has ranked the index they read it by decoding and sorting, with the same answers. The rank-function version is recorded in `schema_meta`, reads trust the column only while it matches, and a writer re-ranks when it is missing or differs. An Open Kioku from before this change can still open, read and write the index: its inserts name no rank, take the column's `-1` default, and a trigger in the file withdraws the recorded version, so reads fall back to decoding and sorting until this version's next writer open re-ranks. `open_kioku_core::graph_edge_window_key` now returns `(rank, edge id)`, and `graph_edge_window_key_with_tier`, added unreleased in #562, is removed.
- Bounded graph edge reads keep proven edges first. `neighbors` (MCP `dependency_path` without `to`, the context pack's graph stream and `dependency_edges`, graph-query traversal), `edges_by_type_for_node` and its paging, `edges_by_type_for_nodes` and `graph_edges_between` cut their window at the lowest edge ids, and edge ids are content hashes, so a heuristic edge could displace a proven one: on this repository's own index, adding symbol-registry edges pushed authoritative resolver `CALLS` edges out of 20-edge windows, including one from `plan_from_context` through `dependency_path`. Every window is now ordered by evidence tier (proven relationships; then `CONTAINS`/`DEFINES` from a parser or index; then corroborated relationships; then heuristic edges, which include regex-extracted containment), then confidence, then edge id, through one core function, `open_kioku_core::sort_graph_edges_for_window`. `shortest_path` enqueues each node's hops in that order, so where equally short routes diverge the stronger hop is tried first. The order of the edges these surfaces return changes, and which edges a window holds changes wherever a node has more edges than the limit; on a file node, parsed `DEFINES` edges now fill a small window ahead of unproven imports, though never ahead of proven imports or dependents. The SQLite store orders and limits these reads in SQL over a persisted rank (see the #574 entry), so their cost follows the window, not the node's degree. MCP `dependency_path` without `to` adds `edges_omitted` when the limit cut edges, and the context graph stream adds a caveat naming the cut; `GraphStore::neighbor_window` (default implementation provided) returns the window with the node's total edge count. Typed questions read by type instead of filtering an untyped window: `ok impact` and MCP `impact_analysis` read each seed's inbound dependency edges type by type (a file with more symbols than the 40-edge window lost its inbound edges to its own `DEFINES` edges, so relationship impacts for large files can grow); MCP `get_references` with `kind: "callers"` or `"callees"` reads `CALLS` by direction, so `has_more` is no longer false for calls past a 500-edge untyped window; `explain_flow` follows each hop's first outgoing `CALLS` edge in window order instead of searching the first 500 `CALLS` edges of the whole graph; and a typed multi-hop graph query reads that type at each hop, which also lets an explicit `DERIVED_FROM` hop match (#562).
- The symbol-registry pass no longer links a name across languages or reads words inside comments and string literals. Its unique-project-name strategy linked a token to the only definition of that name anywhere in the repository, whatever its language, and every strategy read the words of comments and literals as uses, so on this repository's own index a Rust `Utc::now()` was a `CALLS` edge to a `now` function of the demo site's JavaScript, and `lines`, `fs`, `score`, `fail` and `test` linked across Rust, JavaScript, Python and JSON. Tokens are now read from code only, by a lexical pass per language: comment and literal delimiters, raw and multi-line strings, Rust lifetimes, JavaScript and TypeScript regular-expression literals (skipped, so a delimiter inside one hides no code after it), and template interpolations, which stay code. JSON, Markdown and plain text are read whole as before, YAML and TOML lose only their `#` comments, and a double-quoted SQL name stays an identifier. Interpolated Python and Rust strings are not modeled (their names read as literal), and a chunk that starts inside a multi-line string or template reads it as code, after which its closing quote opens a literal and the code that follows reads as literal up to the next quote. The name-only strategies (same module, unique project name, suffix import reachability, fuzzy) never match a symbol of another language family; TypeScript and JavaScript are one family, every other language its own. A same-named definition in another language still counts against a unique-name match, so a name defined in Rust and JavaScript is ambiguous for a Rust token, as before, rather than a match to the Rust definition. Measured on this repository's own index (release builds of `main` and this change over the same tree): registry facts fell from 57,617 to 45,547. 17,292 were removed: 9,659 linked a token to a symbol of another language (none remain), and 7,633 read a word in a comment or string literal. 5,222 were added, every one a code token past the 80th in a chunk that the old reading spent on comment and literal words: 540 same-file, 1,740 direct-import and 2,942 unique-project-name. A review sample judged the added same-file edges right in 14 of 14, direct-import in 12 of 14 and unique-project-name in 9 of 25 (about 60% overall); existing unique-project-name edges sample about the same (about 45%), so the added ones carry the strategy's existing precision rather than a new failure mode (`.contains(..)`, `.expect(..)` and `#[test]` still link to a same-named Rust function elsewhere). Graph edges fell from 59,346 to 52,217, all in registry classes: `REFERENCES` via unique-project-name 21,077 to 14,237, via direct-import 5,404 to 4,895, via same-file 1,223 to 1,028; `CALLS` via unique-project-name 3,575 to 3,310, via direct-import 5,176 to 5,673 and via same-file 1,724 to 1,907. No resolver, SCIP, import or other edge class changed. Registry edges stay heuristic (`open-kioku-symbol-registry/*`, below every proven or corroborated edge in a bounded window). The relationship conformance corpus is unchanged: 336 cases (104 compact) with no false positive, false negative or diagnostic, and the same observation digest. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v13`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#563).
- `ok impact`, MCP `impact_analysis` and the plan's impact report list the files of downstream Rust crates that depend on a changed file's public API, and no longer report keyword matches in Rust crates with no Cargo dependency path to it. The import resolver answers only paths of the importer's own crate, so `use other_crate::Item` reached neither an exact reference nor a relationship edge, and changing `crates/open-kioku-plan/src/lib.rs` listed neither the CLI nor the MCP file that builds a `PlanEngine`, while the plan listed two files of a benchmark fixture tree. Impact now reads the indexed `Cargo.toml` files: a file whose `use` row names a public item of the changed file through the crate name its package uses (renamed dependencies included) or a crate-root `pub use`, in a package that declares the dependency, is a direct impact (`crate_import`), and another file of that package naming the imported item as code (outside comments and string and character literals, tracked across lines, and not as the tail of another crate's path), where the package defines no item of that name and the file imports none from elsewhere, is one too (`crate_import_use`, for `include!`d files and `super::*` imports). Both rank below exact references and above co-change and keyword matches, production files before tests; neither is labelled an exact reference. A keyword match in a Rust file of a package outside the changed package's reverse-dependency closure, or under a workspace manifest in none of its members, is left out; the risk reasons give the count and up to three of the paths, and a file whose manifest is not indexed, is empty or does not parse keeps its match, as does any file of a procedural-macro package, whose emitted code belongs to its users. When the analysis cannot run for the changed file (no manifest covers it, it is not a library module, or it defines no public item), the risk reasons say downstream crates were not measured. The second-hop indirect search no longer searches the stems `lib`, `mod` and `main`. Measured on this repository at `ff0d319c` against `main` (release builds, same index): workflow-benchmark impact recall 0.925 -> 0.975, `ingest-history` 0.50 -> 1.00, `plan-engine` 0.00 -> 0.50 (the plan takes `crates/open-kioku-plan/src/contract_builder.rs` as its impact target there, which `crates/open-kioku-cli/src/commands/mod.rs` does not use), every other case and metric unchanged; retrieval-benchmark and relationship-conformance results identical apart from timings; `ok impact` median wall time over seven runs on five files 0.65/1.13/0.58/0.35/0.25 s before and 0.65/1.17/0.60/0.36/0.24 s after, on one 43-crate repository only (#584).
- `ok index`, both `ok watch` writers and `ok snapshot import` now remove a path the index stops holding (a deleted file, a new `[paths] deny` glob, a secret-like name, vendored or generated code for the semantic corpus) from the semantic vector store and from stored context handles in the same run. Previously the vector store under `.ok/vectors` kept the path's chunk text (`ids.json`), embedding cache entries and vectors on disk until `ok semantic index` rebuilt it, and `.ok/context.sqlite` kept every snippet `ok context --compressed` had stored, which `ok retrieve-context` and MCP `retrieve_context` still served for a denied path. Semantic search itself did not serve the text: any index run changes the fingerprint a semantic generation is checked against, so search already refused the store as stale until rebuilt, and still does. The removed targets are rewritten out of `ids.json` and `embeddings.cache`, each file replaced by a rename, and the generation's vector index (`index.json`, or `index.usearch` and `index.meta.json`) is removed rather than rebuilt, since the store is refused until `ok semantic index` rebuilds it anyway; that rebuild reuses the kept embeddings, so it re-embeds nothing it already had. `ok semantic status` reports such a generation as stale with a rebuild reason naming the removed target and path counts, not as corrupt. A generation that cannot be read, or whose rewrite fails, is removed whole and reported with the reason; interrupted builds are removed, and a `previous` generation left by an interrupted promotion is recovered or removed. `ok semantic status` no longer recovers an interrupted promotion while an index writer holds the lock, and leaves it to that writer. Context handles now record the file they were read from; a handle whose file the index no longer holds is deleted with `PRAGMA secure_delete = ON` and the database compacted with `VACUUM`, and a handle stored before paths were recorded that carries no file range is deleted because it cannot be checked (test handles name no file and are kept). The context store now waits up to 30 s for a concurrent reader or writer instead of failing at once with "database is locked". A removal that fails does not fail the run: it is recorded as `quality.pending_deleted_content_clearing`, which `ok status`, `repo_status` and `ok doctor` report (their wording now names the vector store and context handles), and the next `ok index` or watcher start retries it. `ok index` reports what it removed as an `index[prune]` line, `ok watch` on stderr and `ok snapshot import` as a caveat. New public API: `open_kioku_semantic::prune_vector_store` and `VectorStorePrune`, `ContextHandleStore::prune_removed_paths`, and `open_kioku_watch::derived` (#564).
- `ok index` and `ok watch` no longer leave content moved into an excluded path readable in the index files. A file moved into a `[paths] deny`ed directory, or renamed to a secret-like name (`keys.rs` to `keys.pem`), dropped a path the policy still admits, so the run did not compact and the old rows' bytes stayed in SQLite free pages. The store now keeps two flags in `schema_meta`, naming no path and cleared by a compaction: an indexed path was removed, and the security rules began skipping a path the previous manifest did not record as skipped (compared in memory; no skipped file is read). A run that sets either while the other is set compacts, so a move split over two runs (a copy then a delete, or a delete then a copy, which `ok watch` sees as separate events) is caught too. This is an inference and over-compacts: once a file has been deleted or renamed since the last compaction, the next new denied or secret-like file (such as a new `.env`) costs one compaction, and so does the next delete after one appeared. Edits, deletes and renames within what the policy admits do not compact on their own. Not detected: a move onto an existing denied file of the same name, a rename to a secret-like name while another secret-like file disappears in the same run (withheld paths are compared by count), and a move into a Git-ignored, hidden or `[index] exclude`d path; to have a path's content removed from the index files, use `[paths] deny`, not `.gitignore` (#567).
- `ok index` and `ok watch` no longer leave the content of a path excluded after it was indexed (a new `[paths] deny` glob, a secret-like name) readable in the index files. Its rows were deleted, but the bundled SQLite kept their bytes in free pages, in the unused space of pages still in use, and, while another process such as an MCP server held the index open, in a write-ahead log that was never reset and in database pages it had replaced. The store now deletes with `PRAGMA secure_delete = FAST` (zeroing in pages that stay in use, at no extra I/O); a run that removed a path the current policy excludes compacts the database with `VACUUM`; and every run ends with a truncating WAL checkpoint, which an idle open connection does not block. A database an earlier version wrote is compacted once by the first `ok index` or watcher start and marked in `schema_meta` (`excluded_content_cleared_v1`). Measured on this repository (release builds, five runs each, 262 MB index): a full `ok index` that removes nothing wrote a median 558.6 MB, as without `secure_delete` (558.1 MB), where `secure_delete = ON` wrote 752.3 MB; a run that removed a denied crate compacted in about 5 s and wrote 1,336 MB. A compaction or checkpoint that fails is recorded as `quality.pending_deleted_content_clearing`, reported by `ok status`, `repo_status` and a `deleted_content` warning in `ok doctor`, and retried by the next `ok index` or watcher start (not on each file event). `ok snapshot import` also compacts an artifact without that record, or whose manifest records a pending clearing, whatever its free-page count, and deletes the withheld manifest with `secure_delete` on (#553).
- Search no longer matches a symbol's stored metadata. The Tantivy index indexed each chunk's whole serialized symbol record, with the identifier tokenizer, as a default query field, so its keys and values (`id`, `visibility`, `private`, `public`, `crate`, `confidence`, `high`, `language`, `rust`, `kind`, `function`, `method`, `provenance`, `tree_sitter`, and the scope id's scope-kind and ordinal words) were terms of nearly every chunk that had a symbol: "private key handling" matched every private Rust item, and a change to stored metadata alone (#565) moved BM25 document lengths and the ranking of unrelated queries. A document's symbol is now searched only by its name, qualified name, signature and declaring file path, in a new indexed field, `symbol_text`; the record stays stored for results but is no longer searched. The declaring path is the code-vocabulary part of the scope id: without it, symbol chunks matched their directory and file words once instead of twice, and on the development split of a 10k-file Java corpus two of 51 `issue_to_code` cases lost their gold file from the top 20. Search results, the lexical context stream and plan edit boundaries lose matches that existed only in metadata (the `plan_change` fixture snapshot for "add a subtract function next to add" no longer selects `src/routes.rs`, whose `orders_route` matched only through its stored kind `function`). A search index written before this change has no `symbol_text`: it still opens and is searched as it was written, and nothing reports it as stale, until the next `ok index` (or `ok watch` update), which always rebuilds the Tantivy index from scratch. Measured against `main` at the same base (release builds; commit-derived corpora on the CI runner, one run each): the retrieval fixture's gated fusion holdout R@5 rose from 0.722 to 0.778 with MRR unchanged at 0.806, and fusion dev MRR from 0.772 to 0.781. Searching the declaring path costs the fixture: without it, the same holdout measured R@5 0.833 and MRR 0.889 (−0.055 R@5, −0.083 MRR). It was kept because the commit-derived development splits below favour it (Java `issue_to_code` R@20 0.8039 without it, 0.8431 with it). Gold files, main → this change: in fusion, `go-edit-carrier-contract` 6 → 4, `python-edit-repository-contract` 3 → 2 and `python-issue-order-persistence` 3 → 2 move up, and `java-comment-login-rejection-audit`'s `AuditTrail.java` 3 → 4 moves down within the top 5 (it had matched the query word `id` only through its record's `id` keys). In the ungated lexical strategy the same three move up. `java-comment-login-rejection-audit` goes from [2, 5] to [3, 6], and `ts-trace-invoice-created`'s `taxPolicy.ts` from 4 to 5. `AuditTrail.java` leaving the top 5 of that development case is what lowers lexical dev R@5 from 0.860 to 0.833; these lexical drops likely come from switching to a short symbol field, whose BM25 length normalisation favours short signatures. The baseline is re-frozen. The workflow benchmark's aggregate metrics and every per-case recall are unchanged (context, test, boundary and verdict 1.0, impact 0.9); 19 of its 20 cases reorder or swap entries in their top context, impact or test lists. Commit-derived development splits against `main`: Java (10k files) R@20 0.7519 → 0.7634, MRR 0.4779 → 0.4853, `issue_to_code` R@20 unchanged at 0.8431; Go (~800 files) R@5 0.5714 → 0.5765, R@20 0.7704 → 0.7653, MRR 0.3995 → 0.3957 (`issue_to_code` MRR 0.3382 → 0.3237); TypeScript (~900 files) MRR 0.6564 → 0.6647; Python (~4k files) R@20 0.7208 → 0.7273, MRR 0.4905 → 0.4935. Holdout splits, measured once for reporting: Java MRR 0.5166 → 0.5127, R@20 0.7168 → 0.7257; Go MRR 0.5390 → 0.5564; TypeScript MRR 0.6826 → 0.6771, R@20 0.8802 → 0.8982; Python MRR 0.5397 → 0.5466, R@5 0.6633 → 0.6834. Every gated family is within tolerance of the frozen baselines (#569).
- Rust and Java symbol visibility is read from the declaration's own visibility modifier instead of a substring of its text. A private Rust function whose body contains `pub ` (a test asserting on `"pub fn helper"`, a `macro_rules!` expanding to a `pub fn`) was recorded `public`, and so could be kept as an impact search term as if it were production API; it is now `private`. `pub(crate)`, `pub(super)` and `pub(in path)` items, previously `private` unless their text happened to contain `pub `, are now `crate`, and `pub(self)` is `private`. A package-private Java class or method whose body contains ` public ` (any class with a public member) was recorded `public`; it is now `package`. Trait and interface members, which carry no modifier of their own, are covered by the next entry (#556).
- Rust trait items and trait `impl` members, and Java interface members with no access keyword, are recorded as visible as they are. Read from their own modifier since #556, which they do not have, so they were recorded `private` (Rust) and `package` (Java). An item in a trait body now takes the trait's visibility. A member of `impl Trait for Type` takes the narrower of the trait's and the type's visibility, read from their declarations when the file declares them by that bare name; a trait named by a path (`fmt::Display`, `super::Store`), a prelude or imported trait, or a name the file declares twice with different visibility is not narrowed, so its members are `public`, callable wherever the trait is in scope. Inherent `impl` members keep their own modifier. A Java method, nested type or constant of an interface or annotation type with no access keyword is `public`, and an explicit `private` interface method stays `private`; a member records its own access, not its reach through a package-private or private enclosing interface. Interface constants are not indexed as symbols, so no constant changes. The only visibility consumer, impact term selection, now keeps a trait `impl` method named `test_*` outside a test path as a search term, as it keeps any public symbol with that prefix. On this repository 241 Rust symbols change from `private` to `public` and no other visibility changes. The stored symbol record is part of the lexical index, so BM25 scores move slightly: the retrieval benchmark matches its frozen baseline, the compact relationship benchmark is unchanged, and the workflow benchmark's aggregate metrics are unchanged, with one case (`architecture`) listing a different tenth impact path. The parser semantics version is `tier1-parser-semantics-v6`, so an index built before this change reports `analysis_semantics_status: RebuildRequired` until `ok index` rebuilds it (#565).
- The symbol-registry pass no longer lets the order of a line's tokens decide whether it records a call. It kept one edge per token, line and target, and the first use took it, so in `let path = dir.path();` the bare `path` recorded a `REFERENCES` edge and the `CALLS` edge of `dir.path()` to the same item was dropped, while `dir.path(); path` recorded the call. A call now takes the line's edge over a reference to the same target whichever comes first, so such a line records one `CALLS` edge and no `REFERENCES` edge. The #526 rule that a member use of a name does not take the item a bare use on the same line was ruled out from also holds when the member use is written first (`dir.path().join(path)` inside a `mod tests` that imports `path` from elsewhere); it used to apply only when the bare use came first. The added edges are heuristic like every registry edge (`open-kioku-symbol-registry/*`, High or Medium confidence, dropped in `resolution_mode = "v2"`), and member calls carry no receiver-type evidence, as on lines without a same-named binding. On this repository's own index the change adds 160 registry `CALLS` edges (84 `direct-import`, 30 `same-file`, 46 `unique-project-name`) and removes 1, and removes 39 registry `REFERENCES` edges, 38 of them because the same line's use is now recorded as the `CALLS` edge; no resolver, SCIP, import or other edge class changes. Most added calls are right (`let policy = policy(..)`, `evidence: evidence()`, `self.validation_facts()`), and about a fifth are wrong, chiefly `unique-project-name` matches across languages or in comments (`Utc::now()` to a function of the site's JavaScript), the same matches the registry already makes on lines without a same-named binding. 41 existing edges now cite a different line or evidence id, because the line that used to supply their `REFERENCES` evidence now supplies a `CALLS` edge. The added edges are heuristic, so a bounded edge window ranks them below every proven or corroborated edge and they displace none: `dependency_path` from `plan_from_context` returns the same 20 neighbours as before. In the relationship conformance corpus the Rust `nested_shadowing` case (`let target_fn = || {}; target_fn();` on one line) now observes a heuristic `CALLS` edge to the shadowed `target_fn`, as the Python case, written over two lines, already did; its expected outcome concerns authoritative edges and still passes, and the approved baseline's observation digest is updated for it. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v12`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` until `ok index` (#534).
- `ok impact`, MCP `impact_analysis`, and the impact section of `ok plan` and the context pack no longer spend a file's lexical impact searches on its test code. A file's direct impacts include a BM25 search for each of its eight longest names, so long `#[test]` names and `#[cfg(test)]` helpers took every slot: on this repository all eight terms searched for `crates/open-kioku-ingest/src/lib.rs`, `crates/open-kioku-context/src/lib.rs` and `crates/open-kioku-plan/src/contract_builder.rs` were test names, and adding a test changed which files a change reported as impacted. A name is now test code when it is one of its file's indexed test targets (matched by name and overlapping line range, so a SCIP definition of a test matches too) or lies in the innermost Rust inline module enclosing one; a public symbol recorded as a test only by its `test` name prefix outside a test path, such as `pub fn test_connection_health`, stays a search term. Test code is left out unless a file has no other non-generic names; ordering is unchanged (longest first). Measured on the self-repository workflow benchmark (release builds, same tree): impact recall at 10 unchanged at 0.90; `context-pack` goes 0 to 1 (`crates/open-kioku-plan/src/lib.rs`, which depends on the context crate, moves from 15th to 1st), `ingest-history` goes 1 to 0 (`crates/open-kioku-git/src/lib.rs`, a dependency of the ingest crate rather than a dependent, was reached only through test-name searches), and `plan-engine` stays 0 (its expected file moves from 13th to 12th). The retrieval benchmark matches its frozen baseline, the compact relationship benchmark is unchanged, and all four commit-derived corpora score identically to `main` on every metric and task family (#548).
- A Rust file that no indexed crate root declares is no longer read against the wrong crate when its own crate root was not indexed. With `src/main.rs` skipped (over `max_file_size`, excluded, ignored or unreadable) beside an indexed `src/lib.rs`, a `src/cli.rs` that only `main.rs` declares was read against `lib.rs`, so `crate::helper()` there got an `Exact` `CALLS` edge to the library's `helper` and `use crate::helper;` bound to it, where rustc compiles `cli.rs` into the binary alone. Discovery's skipped files now reach the module tree, and a file of `src/` that no indexed root declares is read against no crate when a crate root of `src/` was skipped, is named by the manifest but was not indexed (a secret-like root is skipped with its path redacted, and the manifest still names it), or is one whose module tree the index does not follow; its `crate::` paths are left unresolved and a `relationship_resolution` quality note gives the number of such roots and files, naming none (`quality.quality_notes` in `ok --json status` and MCP `repo_status`). Without a skipped root such a file (a module declared inside a macro, say) is still read against the indexed `lib.rs`/`main.rs`, as before. A file in `src/bin/`, `tests/`, `examples/` or `benches/` that no crate root declares, such as `src/bin/tool/sub.rs`, which `tool.rs`'s `mod sub;` would look for in `src/bin/`, no longer binds `use crate::...` against that directory's crate roots either; its call paths were already left unresolved (#554).
- Cargo manifest targets are read for crate roots: a `[[bin]]`, `[[test]]`, `[[example]]` or `[[bench]]` `path` is a crate root whose modules live beside it, so with `[[bin]] path = "src/cli.rs"` the `mod util;` it declares places `src/util.rs` and `crate::util::f()` there gets its `Exact` edge, where it was left unresolved. `autobins = false`, `autotests = false`, `autoexamples = false` and `autobenches = false` turn off discovery of that kind: `src/main.rs` and the files directly in `src/bin/` are then not binaries unless a `[[bin]]` names them, by `path` or by `name`, and an unnamed `src/main.rs`, which no crate compiles, is read against no crate rather than as a crate root. A target root in a subdirectory of `src/` (`[[bin]] path = "src/tools/gen.rs"`), whose modules share files with the library's, is not followed; the package is counted in the `relationship_resolution` quality note and the files of `src/` no indexed root declares are read against no crate. Only the table-per-target form is read, not an inline array (`bin = [{ ... }]`) (#544).
- On this repository's own index and on a large async-runtime crate (555 Rust files) no `CALLS` edge changes. In that crate 328 `src/` files are ones the declared module tree does not place (modules declared inside `cfg_*!` macros among them); the 22 exact edges from those files are kept, and would be withheld only if a crate root of its `src/` were skipped. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v9`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` and must be rebuilt with `ok index`. Three Rust `CALLS` conformance cases cover a skipped `main.rs`, an undeclared `src/bin/` module and a `[[bin]] path` crate root (compact count 99) (#544, #554).
- A module that a `[lib] path` root outside `src/` and another crate root beside it both declare is no longer read as the other crate's alone. With `[lib] path = "lib.rs"` and `[[bin]] path = "main.rs"` both declaring `mod util;`, `util.rs` was placed under `main.rs` only, so `crate::helper()` there got an `Exact` `CALLS` edge to the binary's `helper`, although `util.rs` is compiled into both crates and `crate::helper` names a different function in each; the same layout in `src/` already emitted no edge. Such a library root is now a crate root of the module tree of its directory whenever another crate root the index follows keeps its modules there (a `[[bin]]`, `[[test]]`, `[[example]]` or `[[bench]]` `path`, or a discovered `<dir>/<name>/main.rs`), so a module both declare belongs to both crates and gets no authoritative edge, and a module only the library declares is now placed and resolved against the library (`crate::helper()` in it gets its `Exact` edge, where it was left unresolved). A library root alone in its directory is still not followed, and a crate-name path (`mylib::helper`) into a library outside `src/` is still left unresolved; such a package stays counted in the `relationship_resolution` quality note. On this repository's own index and on a large async-runtime crate (555 Rust files) no `CALLS` edge changes. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v10`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` and must be rebuilt with `ok index`. A module path read in a package at the repository root no longer loses the modules below its crate roots: the module files of a crate module tree at `""` were looked up as `/tools/mod.rs` and its items as `::tools::t`, so there `crate::tools::t()` and `super::t()` from `tools/inner.rs` were left unresolved where the same package in a subdirectory resolved them. Two Rust `CALLS` conformance cases cover a module both roots declare and one only the library declares (compact count 101) (#561).
- A Rust module file another crate may also compile no longer gets authoritative edges read against one crate alone. Three layouts gave `crate::helper()` in such a file an `Exact` `CALLS` edge to one crate's `helper` although `helper` names a different function in each crate compiling the file: a `[lib] path` spelled through `..` (`src/../lib.rs`) was not recognised as the `lib.rs` beside `Cargo.toml`, so the #561 fix did not apply to a `util.rs` it and a `[[bin]] path = "main.rs"` both declare; a module the indexed `main.rs` declares was read as the binary's alone when `lib.rs` was skipped (over `max_file_size`, excluded or ignored), although the skipped library may declare it too; and a `util.rs` that `lib.rs` declares with `mod util;` and `main.rs` mounts with `#[path = "util.rs"] mod u2;` was read as the library's alone. A crate root path the manifest names is now placed by its `.`/`..`-normalised form while it stays in the package (one leaving the package is still not followed), so a module only such a library declares now gets its `Exact` edge to the library's item, where it was left unresolved. A module file is now marked as possibly in another crate when it sits below the directory of a crate root the index could not read, or when a `#[path]` attribute in a file of another crate (including a build script or an integration test) mounts it; `crate::`, `self::` and `super::` paths written in it, and `use` paths it imports through, are then left unresolved, while a path from its own crate into it still resolves. `#[path]` targets are now read from the attribute's string literal (also through `cfg_attr`); a `path` inside an inline module is matched below the declaring file's directory, and one the index cannot read marks every file of its package outside the declaring crate. Such files are counted in a new `relationship_resolution` quality note. On this repository's own index, a large async-runtime crate (555 Rust files) and a CLI workspace with a library and binary side by side (244 Rust files) no `CALLS` edge changes at any call site. `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v11`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` and must be rebuilt with `ok index`. Three Rust `CALLS` conformance cases cover the three layouts (compact count 104) (#572).
- A Rust module file another crate may also compile is now marked more narrowly, and in one more case (#576). A `self::` or `super::` path in a marked file that ends below the file's own module (`self::inner::f()`, or `super::inner::f()` from its `mod tests`) gets its `Exact` `CALLS` edge and import binding again: the module files below it are the same files in every crate that declares it with `mod`. It stays unresolved where another crate mounts the file itself with `#[path]` (other than a `mod.rs`), since that crate reads the file's `mod` items from its directory, and where a `#[path]` the index cannot read may mount it; `crate::` paths, and `super::` paths that leave the file's module, stay unresolved as before. A crate root skipped only because it is over `max_file_size` (and at most 16 MiB) now has its text read, with comments and the contents of string and character literals removed, for `mod name;` and `mod name {` declarations, and shares only the modules it names; a root excluded by a path policy (`.okignore`, `.gitignore`, `[index] exclude`, the security policy) is still never read and is taken to declare every module below its directory. A root whose text cannot tell (a `path` attribute that may sit on a `mod`, `mod $name;`, a `mod` followed by anything but a name and `;` or `{` on its line, `include!`, a literal or block comment left open) is taken to declare every module, at any place; a module a macro declares without writing `mod` in the root is not seen. The module files a `#[path]`-mounted file declares below it are now marked too: with `tests/it.rs` mounting `src/sub/mod.rs`, `crate::helper()` in `src/sub/child.rs` got an `Exact` edge to the library's `helper` although rustc compiles that file into the test crate as well; it is now left unresolved. The `relationship_resolution` quality note on such files also counts the `#[path]` attributes the index could not read (a raw string, a macro, a directory on an inline module), each of which marks files across its package. On this repository's own index and on a large async-runtime crate (555 Rust files) no `CALLS` edge changes. On a large CLI package whose `main.rs` declares no modules (244 Rust files, 5,995 exact `CALLS` edges when every file is indexed), excluding `main.rs` by `.okignore` withheld 1,191 of them (about 20%); 10 are now recovered (all calls through a `use self::child::item;` import in a `mod.rs`, into its own child module, including method calls on a type such an import binds), the rest are `crate::` paths and stay withheld. With `main.rs` instead over `max_file_size`, its lines declare no module, so no file is marked and the index is identical to the fully indexed one (all 1,191 recovered). `RELATIONSHIP_RESOLVER_SEMANTICS_VERSION` is now `ri3-relationship-resolver-v17`, so an existing index reports `RebuildRequired` and must be rebuilt with `ok index`. Three Rust `CALLS` conformance cases cover a `self::` and a `super::` path below a module a skipped `main.rs` may declare, and a `crate::` path in a module a `#[path]`-mounted `mod.rs` declares (compact count 124).
- A `#[path]` attribute inside the module subtree of a `#[path]`-mounted Rust file is now followed when marking the files the mounting crate compiles too, each alternative of a `cfg_attr(.., path = "..")` included since the configuration is not known (#604).
